# Sanity & Hallucination Rules

> Authoring guidance: .claude/docs/templates/guidance/game-design-document-guide.md (load per-section as you author — do not read entirely).

> **Status**: Designed
> **Author**: user + systems-designer (primary), game-designer, creative-director; art-director consulted (via /design-system, autonomous; orchestrated run 2026-10-26)
> **Last Updated**: 2026-10-26
> **Last Verified**: 2026-10-26
> **Index position**: Recommended Design Order 19 (IDX row 19). Category Gameplay · Layer Feature · Priority MVP · Effort L. Specialists: systems-designer (primary), game-designer, creative-director; art-director consulted.
> **Implements Pillar**: P4 Sanity Rewrites Reality (primary — this system is the pillar: perception is rewritten as misinformation with a verification economy, never as a stat debuff, and every lie stays falsifiable at a priced cost); **Serves**: P1 The World Never Lies (the consequence channel, the human channel, trails, geometry and the sane palette are closed lists a lie may not remove, alter or add to, and every lie leaks flesh or violet at an edge); P2 Dread Before Chaos (no phantom tell and no false calm at any stage; the drift is capped so lethal friendly harm is preceded by minutes of lies and a victim tell of at least `SAN_TELL_LEAD_S`); P3 The Web Watches (Attention is read one-way and is an exposure input; darkness is never a sanity cost, so going dark stays a pure P3 choice); P5 Deeper Is Richer, Loss Is Not Total (exposure scales with depth, the level rolls back with the world so death is not a cure, and failure costs ship and body, never knowledge)
> **Creative Director Review (CD-GDD-ALIGN)**: APPROVED 2026-10-26

## Summary

This GDD defines how eldritch exposure (depth, Attention events and real horror contact) lowers each player's hidden sanity level, and how that level rewrites perception as falsifiable misinformation rather than a stat penalty. In the MVP that is class (b), false system states on gauges, inspect cards, glyphs, lamp-housing glow sprites and machine sprites, each of which always leaks, never touches the consequence channel and can always be checked at a priced cost on a four-rung verification ladder (the room, a second instrument, a saner crewmate, time); phantom monsters, crewmates drawn as horrors with system-gated lethal friendly harm, and phantom salvage are designed to the same depth behind switches. Read it if you build the Perception Filter, Adaptive Audio, Interaction's harm gate or Save/Load for Sanity, or if you tune pacing and fairness: it carries the lead-time invariants, the ambient floor, 170 acceptance criteria and the open questions.

> **Quick reference** — Layer: `Feature` · Priority: `MVP` · Key deps: `Command & Simulation Layer (designed), Attention System (designed), Interaction System (designed), Suit & Equipment (designed), Save/Load & Versioned Format (designed); soft: Life-Support Graph, Emitter State & Light-Exposure Query, Ship Body & Interior Physics, Ship Section & Layout (all designed); Horror Entities (In Design, draft on disk, horror-entities.md), Web Map & Transit (In Design, draft on disk, web-map-and-transit.md); Perception Filter, Adaptive Audio & Tells, Boarding & Salvage (all designed)`

## Overview

Sanity & Hallucination Rules is the ship's legibility turned against its keeper, as simulation. In one sentence: it keeps one hidden integer per seat, the **sanity level**, that the web's regard and the crew's depth wear down and that rest in a settled home compartment restores, and it turns that level into a small number of **rules for what the player's own senses may be made to say falsely**, always about a surface the world can contradict, always at a price the player can see before paying it. It is a scene-tree-free, integer-only system (H10) with one divisor-12 step hook, `sanity.drift`, three registered state blocks (`sanity.state`, `sanity.ledger`, `sanity.perception`), two Class B mutators that Interaction calls (`record_falsification`, `record_harm_event`) and one SIM query Interaction's `validate` calls (`actor_sanity_below_harm_threshold`). It submits no command and registers no player input.

**The data layer.** Per seat (keyed by PlayerId, the stable seat identity, never a transport peer ID) the system holds a level in milli-sanity-units (`mSU`, `SAN_MAX` 100000 `[PLACEHOLDER]`), the derived **stage** 0 to 3 (0 sane; 1 the instruments; 2 the light; 3 the world's hue, ART 4.4), two orthogonal flags (`tell_on`, the victim-tell bit; `harm_eligible`, the friendly-harm gate, one SIM bit), and a **lie ledger**: the live record of which module-body surface is lying to this seat, how, until when, and what leak it carries. The level moves in one place only (formula D.1): exposure from a closed list of true, simulation-owned inputs (depth, being aboard a foreign body, Attention's phase and tier, real horror contact, and in the dormant co-op slice a witnessed crew death) minus recovery (a settled home compartment, and in the dormant slice a saner crewmate), under a net cap per run so that lethal friendly harm cannot unlock inside minutes. The ledger is Sanity's own SIM state because `PERCEPTION_OUT` is write-only to simulation, including to its own owner's hook (CR-12); the PERCEPTION_OUT block, `sanity.perception`, is a **pure per-seat projection** of the ledger that only the Perception Filter reads. Four hallucination classes are specified: **(a)** phantom entities and hidden real ones, **(b)** false system states, **(c)** crewmates drawn as horrors, with lethal friendly harm system-gated on the *attacker's* level, and **(d)** phantom salvage. Every class passes the same eligibility guard rails (SN-15): the lie targets only the corruptible module-body channel, keeps a live witness on the consequence channel, leaves a second instrument standing, always carries its leak, and grants uniform immunity after a check.

**The player-facing effect.** The player never sees a number, a bar, a ring, a vignette or a word for the state; the game has no noun for it. They meet it as a gauge they have read a hundred times whose needle will not rest and whose glass edge carries a faint violet gleam; as a duct panel reading green while their breath steps shorter and the visor edge mists; as a pump sprite spinning on a dead branch; later as a lamp cone that seems to reach farther than it does, with a fringe at its rim; later still as the world's hue bleeding violet at pipe edges, a thing in the corner of the beam that has no footprint, and, for an actor already deep, a crewmate drawn as a horror who still walks like a technician, holds a true Tungsten beam and speaks in a true voice. They answer by **asking the world** and paying the price: walk to the thing the gauge is about with the lamp on and hear the hiss, read the lamps down the branch, scan with the chargeless scanner, find a second gauge elsewhere, ask a saner crewmate (dormant slice), or wait out the lie, which is the dearest rung because the real fault keeps bleeding the room. No check shows a verdict, a card or a "you were hallucinating" line; the world settles it by being itself. Camera, controls, lamp, aim, movement, prompts, durations and the save and pause surfaces do not change at any stage.

**What the game loses without it.** Pillar 4 is the hook ("misinformation with a verification economy around it", not a stat debuff), and this system is the pillar's only mechanism. Without it, low sanity would be a movement or aim penalty, the exact outcome the pillar's secondary design test rejects ("-10% move speed" against "a gauge showing a false value": the false gauge); the Graph's falsifiability table (LSG LS-22), Suit's chargeless scanner, Interaction's `field_check` and the whole consequence channel would have nothing to be a witness *against*; and the friendly-harm design (death is lethal, including friendly fire, by user decision) would have no gate, no victim tell and no attribution, which is the difference between "a crewmate killed me because the web made them see a monster" and unconstrained PvP, an anti-pillar. The core hypothesis's fifth requirement (a single hallucination class, false system state, falsifiable by the physical world) cannot be tested without it.

**MVP scope.** `CLASSES_ENABLED` is a data bitmask; the MVP enables **class (b) only**. The rules for classes (a), (c) and (d) are written to the same depth and are testable against stubs, but every class-(a), (c) or (d) acceptance criterion is tagged deferred with a named blocker (Horror Entities, Perception Filter, Boarding & Salvage, Co-op). Suit readings stay honest in the MVP (SE-29), the scanner is an honest anchor, and a single seat has no crewmate, so the MVP ladder is rungs 1, 2 and 4 (the room, a second instrument, time). The harm gate bit is computed and queryable in the MVP but has no target.

**Index wording reconciled.** The index rationale (IDX Systems Enumeration bullet 19) is honoured with the corrections below, each recorded as a refresh owed and never as an edit.

| The index says | This GDD does | Why | Refresh owed |
|---|---|---|---|
| "writes per-player perception state keyed by a stable PlayerId (seat)" | writes **three** blocks: the replicable summary `sanity.state`, a HOST_ONLY `sanity.ledger`, and the PERCEPTION_OUT projection `sanity.perception`; all PER_PLAYER, keyed by PlayerId | CR-12 blinds Sanity's own hook to PERCEPTION_OUT, so active-lie state must be SIM; one `repl_hint` per block (CR-8) forces the summary and the ledger apart | kernel Interactions row 10 says "level and perception" (two blocks) and omits `persist_scope`: a wording refresh (SL OQ-21) |
| "receives falsification checks as commands from Interaction/Suit" | receives them as the **Class B mutator** `record_falsification` called inside Interaction's `field_check` apply; Suit sends none (its only command is `equip_tool`); Sanity registers **no** player-origin command | sim systems never subscribe to events (CR-10) and the apply is infallible (CR-9), so a mutator call is the only legal channel; the `field_check` command is Interaction's | kernel row 10 and IA-30 wording ("commands") |
| "No simulation system ever reads perception state — the friendly-harm gate reads the sanity level" | adopted verbatim and made structural: the gate is one SIM bit, `harm_eligible`, and the class-(c) render level **is that bit** (the Filter reads it from SIM; no PERCEPTION_OUT copy exists) | closes IA-31 precondition (1), the harm-prompt leak | none |
| "the four hallucination classes and their guard rails" | four classes, one shared guard-rail set (SN-15) plus a per-class rule (SN-20 to SN-22) | a guard rail is stated once so no class can drift from it | none |

| graph edge Graph -> Sanity and Interaction -> Sanity are absent or soft in the index | both are listed here as dependencies (Graph soft; Interaction Hard) | LSG:867 and IA:824 already state them | index edge fixes (C13) |

**Reading guide.** Detailed Design holds the named rules `SN-1` to `SN-30` (Core Rules), six state tables (stage, victim-tell flag, harm gate, lie lifecycle, compartment-entry `entry_pending`, seat lifecycle) and one Interactions table per neighbour with the dependency GDD's own row quoted. Numbers are `[PLACEHOLDER]` unless a registry name says otherwise; every locked constant (`TICK_RATE`, `GRAPH_STEP_TICKS`, `HP_OK`, `HP_FAIL`, `W_MIN_WARNING_S`, `FIELD_CHECK_TICKS`, `HARM_CHANNEL_TICKS`, `HARM_TOTAL_BP`, `SUIT_EXO_BYPASS_LIMITER`, `DECOY_OVERLAYS_MAX`, `WEIGHT_TELL_MIN_PM`, `MAX_DIRTY_KEYS`, `STEP_BUDGET_MS`) is cited by name with its registry value and never restated with a different one. Where this GDD adds a rule the orchestrator's rulings left open it carries an `(orchestrator ruling: ...)` note; where it adopts a specialist proposal that amends a ruling it is listed in Open Questions rather than silently applied. Neighbours with no GDD, and Horror Entities and Web Map & Transit (In Design, drafts on disk), are marked `(provisional — <dep> undesigned/in flight)`.

## Player Fantasy

**Your eyes are an instrument you cannot recalibrate.** You are a technician keeping a dead ship alive, and the hardest instrument to keep true is your own sight. The power you earn is not strength. It is knowing when to doubt what you see, and being able to afford to check.

The concept states the horror precisely: "The horror is not that the web attacks the ship. It is that the web attacks the crew's ability to know whether the ship is fine." Sanity here is therefore not a second resource beside the ship. It is the ship's legibility turned against its keeper. Most survival games hand the player a bar to protect, a thing the game does *to* them. Here the player owns no number and suffers no penalty. What they own is a **verification economy** (the concept's phrase: "misinformation with a verification economy around it"), and their competence is measured in how cheaply and correctly they can ask the world a question. The competence the game promises at its core is "learning when to doubt your eyes", and this section makes that clause the emotional centre of the system.

**Experience targets (concrete moments, not adjectives).**

1. *The glance that snags.* A gauge the player has read a hundred times has a needle that will not rest where a sane needle would. Nothing announces it. They have time to notice.
2. *The walk to the pipe.* They walk, lit, to the thing the gauge is about, and the world answers in its own language (a hiss, a draught, frost) without any interface confirming it.
3. *The cost they chose.* They decide whether this lie is worth two seconds and a tool swap, a walk, or a wait, and they can see the price before they pay it.
4. *Quiet certainty.* After the check they know. The gauge may still be lying. That is the point: knowledge and the lie are different things.
5. *(Deferred, VS-B)* *The friend who walks like a technician.* See the second anchor below.

### Anchor moment (MVP, solo, class b): "The needle that won't hold still"

Built from fault 1 of the MVP fault set (an O2 pinhole at a clamp, an exposed DUCT LEAK; LSG LS-26). Every true side of this scene sits on the consequence channel (LSG LS-14) or on a Suit body cue; the lie sits only on the module-body gauge and glyph.

| Beat | What the player sees and hears | What is true | What the player does | What it costs |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1. Lit entry | The chest lamp sweeps in. The duct gauge reads in the OK band, and a violet gleam sits on the glass edge. The needle is not at rest: it glides slowly and never settles (the GLIDE signature, ART HR-5). | The duct has a pinhole leak. The gauge is module-body, so it is corruptible (LSG LS-14). | Nothing yet. The player may or may not look at the glass edge. | Zero. The lamp was already on; lit entry is not a cost. |
| 2. The snag | A faint sound signature accompanies the needle (Adaptive Audio's family, disjoint from the tell, SN-25). Separately, the player's breath steps shorter and the visor edge begins to mist: Suit body cues, never corruptible (SE-25). | The room's O2 band is falling. The Suit cue is a true reading of the room. | The player notices the disagreement between the numeral and their breath. This is the stage-1 teaching beat. | Attention to notice. No game resource. |
| 3. Choose a rung | Honest options exist, each with a visible price (rung table below). | The truth is available on three rungs in the MVP: the room, a second instrument (an honest `suit.o2` reading of the room, another fixed gauge, or the scanner), time. | The player picks one. | See table. |
| 4. The room rung | Walking to the duct anchor the player hears the HISS and feels the AIRFLOW draught; mist may form at the pinhole. | The pinhole is real and has been all along. | Walk to the duct with the lamp on and listen. | About 3.2 s for 8 tiles (384 px at `V_WALK` 2 px/tick and `TICK_RATE` 60), walk noise only (`NOISE_PER_TICK` walk 1), lamp on (light is an Attention input). |
| 5. Certainty without ceremony | No icon, no card, no "you were hallucinating" line. The hiss simply is. | The gauge may still read OK with its gleam. The lie has not ended; the player's belief has. | Repair the pinhole (a real verb) or leave. | Repair time and noise per the repair verb (Interaction's). A completed repair or inspect on the subject delivers its true reason as the ordinary outcome after the full channel and also collapses the lie as a "room" rung (SN-17). |

**What this beat proves.** The player was never told anything. The sane palette told them *where* to look (violet at the glass edge), the body told them *something* was wrong (breath and visor), and the world told them *what* (hiss, draught). If they ignored all three and trusted the numeral, the pinhole bleeds the room: a false "fixed" state on a scrubber is a death walked into. That is the Pillar 4 contract in one beat: fair, cheap to check, costly to ignore.

**Rung price table for this anchor.** The order is the Graph's (LSG:44): the room itself; a second instrument elsewhere on the ship; a saner crewmate (VS-B); time. The full per-fault table is formula D.7.

| Rung | MVP-live? | Cost in time | Light | Noise | Tool swap | Trust | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1. The room | Yes | the walk to the duct, about 3.2 s per 8 tiles | lit (the lamp is already on) | walk noise | none | none | The witness is guaranteed by rule L-W (SN-15): a FALSE_OK lie is only eligible while the hiss and draught are active |
| 2. A second instrument | Yes | an honest `suit.o2` reading of the seat's current compartment is free to read; another fixed gauge on the loop costs a walk (D.7); the scanner costs `FIELD_CHECK_TICKS` (2 s) of lit channel | lit for the channel | Interaction's declared class | scanner: equip slot 3 (no registered duration, OQ-SN-6) | none | Rung 2 always exists (rule L-2I, SN-15) |
| 3. A saner crewmate | No (VS-B) | conversation | none | voice | none | the cost is **trust**: believing a friend's word over one's own eyes | The human channel is never faked |
| 4. Time | Yes | wait out the lie's life, at most `LIE_LIFE_S` max `[PLACEHOLDER]` | n/a | n/a | none | none | Expensive by design: the real leak keeps bleeding the room while you wait. This is the rung that teaches rungs 1 and 2 are worth paying for |

### Second anchor (DEFERRED, VS-B, class c): "The friend who walks like a technician"

Not the headline; the MVP ships without it. It is recorded so the MVP's rules deepen into it rather than being replaced by it. A crewmate stands at a console, seen by an actor whose `harm_eligible` bit is set. They are drawn as a horror: a branching silhouette, iridescence, a violet fringe. But the rig is driven by the crewmate's real velocity, so it is "a horror that walks like a technician" (ART 5.5). They carry their **true Tungsten beam in its true shape** and speak in their **true voice**; the art bible names these as three redundant tells (beam shape, fringe, voice), any one of which suffices. The actor's cutter is in hand. Nothing happens unless the actor deliberately starts a lit `HARM_CHANNEL_TICKS` channel with a cutter or welder; a hallucination alone harms nobody. If they do, the act is lethal (user decision: "This is like project zomboid, death is a bitch. It happens."; `SUIT_EXO_BYPASS_LIMITER` is 1 in the registry) and the death is attributed to the web. The victim has been shown a violet fringe for at least `SAN_TELL_LEAD_S` before eligibility. The fantasy line here is **not** "you killed your friend". It is: *the beam was theirs, the voice was theirs, and you swung anyway because the shape was wrong.* The player had the truth channel and did not read it. That is a fair, devastating, retellable story, which is the playtest signal the index asks for ("friendly-fire deaths told as stories and crews keep playing together"). It is a story-generating failure, not a power, and the section does not romanticise it.

### Pillar text served (verbatim)

- P4 (the pillar): "Low sanity changes what the player perceives, not just their stats." The class (b) line: "False system states. Broken reads as fixed, fixed as broken; the lie extends from the gauge to the sprite. The physical consequence (P1) stays honest."
- P4 verification clause: "**No lie is unfalsifiable**: the truth is always available through the physical world, a second instrument, a saner crewmate, the human channel, or time — and checking costs time, risk or trust. At least one channel of truth always remains. Solo play is supported through the first two checks; co-op adds the human ones."
- P4 design tests: "a hallucination that can never be disproved vs. one that costs time, risk or trust to disprove → the costly one." Secondary: "−10% move speed vs. a gauge showing a false value → the false gauge."
- P1, the clause that makes the fantasy bearable: "A pipe may *look* fixed, but the room still gets cold, the air still thins, the pressure still drops." and "Subtle is allowed; absent is not."
- Intended tension: "Pillar 1 (legible) vs. Pillar 4 (lying). Resolved inside the game world: the physical ship is ground truth; instruments and perception are what can be wrong. The tension is the game."

### Design test sentence (this system's own)

> **A lie the game confirms with a verdict (an icon, a card, a "you were hallucinating" line, a cut to the truth) vs. a lie the world settles by being itself (a hiss, a draught, a mist) → the world settles it.**

Any proposed debunk presentation is run through this. If the player learns the truth from something the interface *says* rather than something the world *does*, it fails. It is the sanity-side twin of P1's "hidden stat vs. a visibly leaking pipe → the pipe" and it excludes the reveal register the art bible deliberately does not define. A corollary for tuning: *"it wasn't really broken" vs. "I should have looked at the edge" → the second.* A lie that leaves a player feeling cheated has failed; one that leaves them feeling they skipped a free look has succeeded.

### How the fantasy deepens, not breaks, under hallucination

**The ladder is the competence loop.** Each lie is a question with a priced answer. Stage 1 teaches the first rung (look at the gauge's edge, then at the room). Stage 2, when "the light lies", teaches that the *tool* of looking can be false, so the player re-learns the one anchor that never shifts: the physical radius their light actually reveals when they walk into it. Stage 3 teaches that cross-checking is itself a skill: the player who has paid small prices for twenty minutes is the player who survives the dense stage. Competence grows exactly as lies grow.

**What keeps it from breaking** (each is a rule; SN-15 and SN-17):

1. *Every lie has a witness.* A FALSE_OK lie needs an active consequence-channel witness; a FALSE_FAIL lie needs an honest positive cue of health. Absence alone does not qualify. The player is never asked to disprove a lie with nothing.
2. *A second instrument always exists.* For one seat, no loop may have every instrument lying at once.
3. *Every lie leaks.* Nothing is a seamless lie.
4. *Immunity after checking.* A checked subject is immune to a re-lie for that seat for `DEBUNK_IMMUNE_S`, whether or not it was lying, so a check is never a trap and a clean check leaks nothing.
5. *No reveal register.* Debunking is quiet. The true reason arrives only as the ordinary outcome after the full channel, never instantly, never as a card, never as a cut-to-the-monster.
6. *Certainty is not cure.* A checked lie is not removed from the gauge; the player simply stops being fooled. The sanity level is unchanged by a falsification. The checked gauge carries **no mark** of having been checked: a mark would be a verdict icon in disguise, and the player's memory is the instrument. *(Orchestrator ruling: adopted from the creative-director's risk 3.)*

**Death inside the fantasy.** MVP death handling is the Save/Load death stub: every bound seat DEAD reloads the last autosave, with no beat, no lights-out, no death screen. The level rolls back with the world (SL H3): death is not a sanity cure, and a player already low wakes still low. Identical actions after a death replay the same lie at the same moment, priced by a death, as the scrubber that fails at minute four is in the physical world (SN-24). Knowledge survives in the player's head and later in the Codex: Pillar 5, "failure costs ship and body, never knowledge". The emotional shape is Zomboid's: death is a bitch, it happens, and the lesson survives.

**Under co-op (VS-B, designed, dormant).** Per-seat independence: two players at different stages see different gauges, and the disagreement itself becomes the talk ("is that scrubber really green on your screen?"). The fantasy gains Fellowship without changing its spine: the third rung is the first with a second human in it.

### INTENSE, and "Doubt, not horror"

The user requires hallucinations to be INTENSE: phantom monsters, false broken and fixed states, teammates as monsters, phantom loot. The art bible's emotional target for the lie is "*Doubt, not horror.* The player should feel 'that's not right' before 'that's a monster'. The overlay never adds a mood of its own." These are different axes, not a conflict. **Intense in count, kind and reach; never in tempo, volume or screen-wide treatment.** Intensity is how many lies run at once, how far a lie reaches (gauge, then glyph, then the whole machine sprite at stage 3, "the lie extends from the gauge to the sprite"), how close a phantom comes, and what a believed lie costs. Doubt is the per-lie loudness and tempo: each individual lie is quiet, plausible and leaks at an edge, and the overlay's "energy" is unchanged from the host state, or the player would read sanity off the screen's energy instead of off the world. Stage 3 is the intense register (loop hues bleed violet, flesh warmth enters surfaces) and stays inside the flash clamp (PIPE PR-13, at most 3 flashes in a rolling 1 s). Intensity is never bought by touching a witness, adding a consequence-channel item (a false-broken sprite adds no scorch, no arc POP, no mist, no trail), or removing a leak. If playtest says "not intense enough", the dials are `LIES_MAX`, `LIE_GAP_S`, `ONSET_PM` and the stage thresholds, never a louder lie. *(Orchestrator ruling: the user's INTENSE wins on count and reach; the art bible's unchanged energy is a hard limit on tempo.)*

### Tone and language

Write like the art bible: concrete, physical, sparse. Preferred nouns: lie, leak, witness, edge, rung, reading, the room, the glass, the fringe. Preferred verbs: check, listen, follow, look at the edge, settle, hold, drift. The lie's default flavour is **comfort** (the art bible's "the lie is kinder than the truth": warm, soft, domestic, where the truth is cold and hard; "warmth that was not paid for with a Tungsten source is a symptom"), so comfort-kinds are weighted above fright-kinds at stages 1 and 2 and fright-kinds rise at stage 3 (`KIND_W[kind][stage]`, SN-16). **Banned in GDD prose and in any player-facing string:** meter, bar, a gauge of sanity, debuff, buff, insanity effect, jump scare, reveal, "you were hallucinating", "the truth is revealed", cure (sanity is recovered by settling, not cured), panic as a stat, screen shake as a sanity treatment. The internal integer `mSU` may be named in rules and formulas and is described as never reaching a player surface. A player-facing noun for the state does not exist; characters do not say "I'm going insane". The game's silence on the word is itself a design statement.

### Reference techniques

| Reference | ADOPT | REJECT and why |
|---|---|---|
| *Beyond the Aquila Rift* | The lie is warm and comfortable, the truth cold and hard; the tell is in the almost-right; unpaid warmth on steel as the lie's palette | The explicit reveal shot; the arachnid creature (forbidden by the art bible's no-bilateral-limbs rule); skin as a subject; eroticism as lure |
| *Pandorum* | Waking into wrongness; the hands-free chest lamp as the player's only truth tool (user decision) | Revealing the delusion as a plot twist; authored story beats (anti-pillar) |
| *Project Zomboid* | "Death is a bitch. It happens." Death as raw story material; patient, systemic dread | Skill-grind and kill rewards (anti-pillar) |
| *Eternal Darkness* | The idea that the game itself can feel unreliable | Fourth-wall effects (fake save deletion, fake crash). **Forbidden:** save and pause surfaces are never a hallucination class (SL H6) and the HUD frame is an anchor |
| *Amnesia*, *Don't Starve*, most survival sanity systems | The fear of a state you cannot see | A visible sanity bar; sanity stat penalties; screen-wide blur or vignette; a wobble that tells the player the number (violates IA-39, the P4 secondary test, SE-27) |
| Scripted-scare hallucination games | none | Authored or scripted lies and jump scares: the anti-pillar "not an authored story", and a cheap-death risk |
| *Alien: Isolation*, *Subnautica* | Audio-first dread, tells before threats (Pillar 2) | Phantom tells and false calm: **permanently rejected** (AT-17; OQ-AT-11 closed at the gate); Sanity never fakes an Attention tell or a calm |
| *Barotrauma* | The crew as each other's check on reality | Chat-only trust (VS-B only; the MVP solo has rungs 1, 2 and 4) |
| Apollo console / *Satisfactory* (art bible) | State lives in the sprite: frame, reading, number | Icon-only state; a verdict glyph for hallucination checks (IA F-3: the scanner result is "a reading, never a verdict icon") |

### Anti-pillar and guard-rail statements this system carries

| # | Guard rail | Binds | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| G1 | **No self-harm lever.** No verb, item or action lowers sanity. Falsification grants certainty, not sanity; recovery has no light or power condition. | SN-5, SN-6, SN-8, SN-17; anti-pillar "not a combat game" | CON risk on deliberate self-lowering |
| G2 | **No reward path for harm.** Harm earns the actor nothing: no level change, no XP, no loot, no recovery. | SN-13; anti-pillar "no intentional PvP" | IA-31 "No reward path" |
| G3 | **Harm is system-gated and web-attributed.** Possible only while the *attacker's* `harm_eligible` bit is set (one SIM bit, the same bit the class-(c) render reads), preceded by a victim tell of at least `SAN_TELL_LEAD_S`, recorded in `incidents` attributed to the web. | SN-10, SN-11, SN-13, SN-20 | CON P4(c) |
| G4 | **Harm is lethal by user decision.** `SUIT_EXO_BYPASS_LIMITER` is 1 (registry). The down-only fallback is that one Suit knob, set by the creative-director on the IDX:280 playtest signal; Sanity adds no second switch. | SN-12 | user decision |
| G5 | **No authored lies, no scripts.** Onset is rule-driven from the player's own actions; no authored hallucination records exist in any template. | SN-16, SN-24; DA-37 | CON anti-pillar |
| G6 | **No win state via sanity.** No completion, mastery tier or achievement surface; scars are a later system and never violet or flesh. | anti-pillar "not a game you win" | CON; ART 5.3 |
| G7 | **Darkness is never a sanity cost** (creative-director HARD CONSTRAINT): no unlit seat, unlit room, beam-off or "alone in the dark" input; recovery has no lamp condition. | SN-6, SN-8 | EM:95, EM:1117, LO-31 |
| G8 | **No sanity surface anywhere.** No number, bar, ring, icon, colour, vignette or HUD glyph on any surface, own or crewmate's; no self-tell on the body. The only signs are the world leaking at edges and the one-bit prompt. | SN-14 | ART 5.3, ART 7.0, SE-25, IA-39 |
| G9 | **No stat penalty from sanity.** No movement, aim, channel, damage or stamina value reads sanity. | SN-27 | PCI:42, PC-12 |
| G10 | **The consequence channel, the human channel, trails, geometry, anchors and Attention outputs are never touched.** A lie may not remove, alter or add to them. | SN-15 (L-C), SN-27 | LSG LS-14 |
| G11 | **Class (b) only in the MVP.** Classes (a), (c), (d) are designed to the same depth and gated by `CLASSES_ENABLED`; Suit readings stay honest. | SN-28, SN-19 | CON MVP definition |

*(Orchestrator ruling, framing: three framings were weighed. A, "your eyes are an instrument you cannot recalibrate", is adopted as the spine because it is complete in the solo class-(b) MVP and deepens under co-op. B, "crewmates are each other's instruments", is the co-op deepening, dormant until VS-B. C, "the ship is a body turning against you", is demoted to a register note for the stage-3 look, because it is a palette idea rather than a verification economy.)*

## Detailed Design

### Core Rules

Format: **Rule / Why / Edge notes**. Ids `SN-n` (the prefix is unused in any GDD or the registry; `HR-` is the art bible's id for its horror-rules and is not this system's). Units: one **run** is one `sanity.drift` invocation, `SANITY_DRIFT_DIVISOR` 12 ticks = 0.2 s (5 Hz), where `SANITY_DRIFT_DIVISOR` equals the kernel divisor 12 by relation (KRN:54, KRN:202) and is not a knob (the `SUIT_HP_FULL` precedent); 1 s = 5 runs; `mSU` is the milli-sanity-unit, an integer that never reaches a player surface. Every Sanity-owned number is `[PLACEHOLDER]` and registered as such; every cited constant carries its registry value and owner. "Seat" means a PlayerId (1 in single-player).

#### Block, hook and level

**SN-1. Three blocks, not one.**
*Rule.* Sanity registers exactly three state blocks. Each carries `schema_version` 1, `supported_versions` [1], a plain-data snapshot (no `Vector2i`, no `StringName` keys, no derived caches), explicit dirty keys, restore-idempotence, `default_state`, `post_restore`, and a two-phase `restore` returning `(ok, reason, detail)` from a closed `SANITY_RESTORE_*` reason set; an out-of-range stored value is a hard failure and is never clamped (SL-15, items 1 to 12). `persist_scope` is stated explicitly on both SIM blocks, which closes SL OQ-21 and satisfies the boot lint `SL_BOOT_SCOPE_MISSING`.

| block_id | class | scope | persist / persist_scope | repl_hint | restore_after | contents |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `sanity.state` | SIM | PER_PLAYER (PlayerId) | true / **WORLD** | `SHARED_SUMMARY`; the projection is `{stage, tell_on, harm_eligible}` only | `[]` | `level` (mSU), `stage`, `stage_dwell`, `tell_on`, `harm_eligible`, `harm_below_runs`, `pending`, `last_tell_epoch`, `forced` (dev diagnostic, SN-28; outside the projection; always false in a release export) |
| `sanity.ledger` | SIM | PER_PLAYER | true / **WORLD** | `HOST_ONLY` | `[sanity.state, graph.state, layout.state]` and, *(provisional — Horror Entities In Design, draft on disk, horror-entities.md; block `horror.state`, HE-3)*, `horror.state` | `lies[]` {slot, subject_ref, kind, params, state, onset_stage, start_run, active_run, end_run, debunked_run, leak_modes, first_lie}, `immune[]` {subject_ref, until_run}, per-subject lie visit counters (stream `"sanity.lie/<PlayerId>/<stable_id>"`, a map pruned on subject destruction), RNG counters (`sanity.lure`, `sanity.phantom`, `sanity.hide`), `lit_prev[]`, `entry_pending`, `debounce[]`, `repaired_recent[]`, `horror_enc[]`, `lures[]`, `lies_seen`, the `incidents` ring |
| `sanity.perception` | PERCEPTION_OUT | PER_PLAYER | **false** (derived) | `OWNER_PRIVATE` | none (rebuilt in `post_restore`) | the per-seat projection of the ledger (SN-23) |

*Why.* CR-12 blinds Sanity's own hook to `PERCEPTION_OUT`, so active-lie state must be SIM; a block carries exactly one `repl_hint`, and the level's summary must reach every client (the victim must see the attacker's tell) while the ledger must never reach another client. Splitting by `repl_hint` turns "no reader but Sanity and DIAGNOSTIC" into a block-level lint instead of a field-level one. *(Orchestrator ruling R2, ADR candidate: ledger placement.)*
*Edge notes.* The ledger is a deliberate Pillar 4 oracle risk: a sim system that read it would learn which surface lies. The lint is "no symbol of `sanity.ledger` is read outside `sanity/` except by the Debug Overlay as DIAGNOSTIC" (an AC in Acceptance Criteria). `lit_prev`, `entry_pending` and `debounce` are state, not caches: if rebuilt in `post_restore` from the world at the snapshot tick, a snapshot taken between hook runs could rebuild a different set than the uninterrupted run held, and the kernel's `state_hash` (G4, CR-13) would diverge. PlayerId keys are stable seat ids, never `peer_id` (KRN:52, 100). *Closed reason sets (QA-11).* `SANITY_BOOT_*` (registry: `SANITY_BOOT_REASONS`), one per assertion: `LEAD_INVARIANT` (D.4 (i) to (iv)), `AMBIENT_FLOOR_MISSING`, `AMBIENT_FLOOR_INVARIANT` (D.8 (a) to (c)), `HOME_REFUGE_INVARIANT` (D.8 (d)), `SUB_CAP_INVARIANT` (D.8 (e)), `HARM_HYSTERESIS` (D.9), `IMMUNITY_INVARIANT` (`DEBUNK_IMMUNE_S >= max(LIE_GAP_S)`), `REC_MAX_INVARIANT` (`REC_MAX = REC_SETTLED + REC_CREW`), `OVERLAY_BUDGET` (`PHANTOMS_MAX` plus decoy lies above `DECOY_OVERLAYS_MAX`), `LIE_TARGET_DATA` (a `lie_targets.json` surface outside the module-body channel, a seam-lane valve or breaker entry (MSA F1), or a lie kind with no leak), `HIDE_STANDOFF` (`HIDE_SAFE_R` at or below Horror's `LURK_STANDOFF_PX`, SN-22). `SANITY_RESTORE_*` (registry: `SANITY_RESTORE_REASONS`), one per failure class: `SCHEMA_UNSUPPORTED`, `SHAPE` (missing or extra key), `LEVEL_RANGE`, `STAGE_RANGE`, `COUNTER_RANGE`, `LIE_KIND_UNKNOWN`, `LIE_TIME_ORDER` (not `start_run <= active_run <= end_run`), `SUBJECT_UNRESOLVED`. A boot reason refuses boot; a restore reason fails that block's two-phase restore and leaves the pre-restore state unchanged.

**SN-2. One hook and a fixed run order.**
*Rule.* One hook: `(hook_id sanity.drift, owner sanity, divisor 12, offset 0, after: [graph.propagate.failure, attention.step, emitter.place, body.integrate])`. All four `after` ids exist on disk (LSG, ATT:129, Emitter State, BDY:140); an unknown id or a cycle fails boot (CR-3). Sanity registers no command, no `INPUT_SURFACE`, no PLAYER-origin anything in the MVP; its only writes are inside its own hook and its two Class B mutators. For each bound seat in ascending PlayerId, a run does, in this order: (1) snapshot inputs (Attention view, Graph reads, the seat's section and position, the lit set, `body_is_home`, `ring`); (2) D.1 drift: exposure, recovery, pending drain, level update; (3) D.2 stage, then the `tell_on` flag and D.3 harm gate; (4) ledger upkeep: PENDING to ACTIVE activations with witness re-check, EXPIRED, INVALIDATED, pruning of `immune[]`; (5) lit-entry events then D.5 onset (class b, and a, c, d if enabled); (6) rebuild `sanity.perception` in full from steps 3 to 5; (7) store `lit_prev` for the next run.
*Why.* Reads must see this tick's Graph bands, Attention phase, lit set and seat section at the coincidence `T mod 12 = 0`; the Graph, Attention and Suit hooks (divisor 6) all land on that tick, so Sanity's work is O(seats x small constant). `STEP_BUDGET_MS` 4 is a logging threshold shared with them, not a cap. Offset 0 versus 6 is the kernel owner's to rebalance if the coincident hooks breach it.
*Edge notes.* Conflict C6: the kernel's fixture label `sanity (12, 0)` (KRN:787) is an example, not this registration; the real id is `sanity.drift` and a wording check is routed to the kernel owner (reflexion P-10: examples are read as contracts). Hooks may submit SYSTEM commands; Sanity submits none. `horror.step` runs at divisor 5 (HE-4) and is deliberately not in `after:`; at a Sanity run `horror.view` may be up to 4 ticks old (at most 4 x `V_HUNT_MPX`/1000 px of motion), which E3's 288 px radius and the HIDE collapse tolerate; Sanity never needs same-tick Horror state *(provisional — Horror Entities In Design, draft on disk)*.

**SN-3. Level, units and seat lifecycle.**
*Rule.* `level` is an integer in `[0, SAN_MAX]` mSU, `SAN_MAX` 100000 `[PLACEHOLDER]`, set to `SAN_MAX` for a seat on its first bind in NEW_GAME. A seat first bound mid-game (VS-B join) starts at `SAN_MAX`; a PlayerId that already has stored state keeps it (no join-to-reset lever). Seat lifecycle (table in States and Transitions): an **unbound** seat does not drift, has no onset and retains its state under its PlayerId; a **DOWNED** seat drifts (exposure is real while down) and keeps onset; a **DEAD** seat freezes at the tick of death, and in the MVP death is the death stub (SL-7: reload the last autosave), so the frozen state is replaced by the restored one.
*Why.* Integer and JSON-safe (SL S-1), far below 2^53; the resolution (1 mSU per run) is below the slowest rate; the number never reaches a player (IA-39: "no sanity meter or number exists"). Death is not a cure (SL H3).
*Edge notes.* A guest joining a host world and whether a guest keeps a stage are Co-op's and Sanity Scars' (OQ-SN-4). *Integer conventions (QA-10; every boundary test needs the side of the equality).* On first bind `stage_dwell` is `STAGE_DWELL_RUNS` (saturated), `harm_below_runs` and `pending` are 0, and `last_tell_epoch` is set to the live `tell_epoch`, so a stale BREACHED outcome at bind grants no startle. D.2 reads `stage_dwell` as stored by the previous run and the counter is updated after it (+1, saturating, or 0 on a change), so the first legal change is the run after the counter reaches 25. All time windows (immunity `until_run`, `REPAIR_BIAS_S`, `HORROR_ENC_RESET_S`) are exclusive of their end run (immune while `run < until_run`; biased while `run - stamp < 300`); `LIE_GAP_S` is inclusive (a gap of 225 runs passes at exactly 225); distance tests (`HORROR_NEAR_R`, `HIDE_SAFE_R`, `CREW_R`) are inclusive (`d <= R` counts).

**SN-4. Stages 0 to 3 with hysteresis and dwell.**
*Rule.* Stage is a pure function `stage_of(level, prev_stage, stage_dwell)` (D.2). Stage 0 sane; 1 the instruments; 2 the light; 3 the world's hue (ART 4.4) (registry: `sanity_stage`). Entry thresholds, strictly below: 1 at 70000, 2 at 45000, 3 at 25000 `[PLACEHOLDER]`; exit thresholds, strictly above: entry plus `STAGE_HYST` 2000 (72000, 47000, 27000). A change is blocked until `stage_dwell` has reached `STAGE_DWELL_RUNS` 25 (5 s). Harm eligibility and the victim tell are orthogonal flags (SN-11), never stages. **There is no stage 4.**
*Why.* The art bible has exactly three lie stages; PIPE:904 makes stage 3 the Filter's maximum; PIPE and PCI fixtures use 0 to 3. The SIM steps instantly; visual onset smoothing is the Filter's, subject to PR-13.
*Edge notes.* Conflict C1 routed to Failure & Trail: FT AC-88 "stages 0-4" must read "0-3". Two thresholds crossed in one run is impossible under the cap (36 mSU per run against gaps of at least 20000) and arises only through a restore or debug force, where the stored stage is kept and a forced recompute moves directly to the target.

#### Inputs to the level

**SN-5. Exposure: a closed list of four true inputs.**
*Rule.* Exposure per run is `E = E1 + E2 + E3 + E4`. All are SIM values, true, read one way.
- **E1, depth and foreign body.** `RING_EXPOSURE[ring(p)]` (three depth rings 1 to 3, ring 1 the shallowest, Attention AT-6; `ring(p)` is `ring_of(actor_section(actor))`, and an unknown ring resolves to ring 1) plus `ABOARD_EXPOSURE` while the seat's body is not the home body (home-ness is `body_is_home`, orthogonal to ring, because the home ship moves). E1 is subject to the ambient floor (SN-29). Web Map & Transit publishes `ring(PlayerId)`, `body_is_home(PlayerId)` and `locus_kind(PlayerId)` as SIM reads and states the baseline, ring 1 for HOME, derelict A, derelict B and the pod (WM-27) *(provisional — Web Map & Transit In Design, draft on disk, web-map-and-transit.md)*. **In the pod** (the seat's `loco_state` is IN_POD, or its body is the pod): `aboard` is false, so `ABOARD_EXPOSURE` is not charged; the ring is Attention's `held_ring` (WM-27), and `settled` is false. The hop is neither a refuge nor a drain beyond its ring term. This answers OQ-WM-6 (orchestrator ruling, logged).
- **E2, Attention.** SN-7.
- **E3, real horror contact.** The seat's foot origin is within `HORROR_NEAR_R` 288 px `[PLACEHOLDER]` of the `pos_px` of a registered HORROR subject on the same body, with **no light condition**; the rate is `EXP_HORROR` per run while true, paid against a per-encounter budget (SN-30). Sanity computes this itself from `horror.view` positions; Horror publishes the facts and computes no exposure (HE-32, HE-40, AC-HE-101). Phantoms are never registered (SN-22) and so never feed E3. *(provisional — Horror Entities In Design, draft on disk, horror-entities.md.)*
- **E4, witnessed crew death.** VS-B, dormant: value 0 until Co-op exists.
*Why.* Legible, true, and every input already designed or provisional. The MVP target-derelict sortie drifts sanity even at Attention tier 0, through the aboard term, which is what lets the class-(b) hypothesis be tested in a 30-minute session.
*Edge notes.* E3 is **distance only** (checkpoint CQ7, closes OQ-SN-3): SN-6 forbids any light condition in an exposure term, and a lit-required E3 would turn darkness into a sanity reward and double-count Attention's restraint, so dousing the lamp near a horror neither raises nor stops E3. E2 is ship-global (every bound seat) in the MVP, which is moot with one seat; scoping E2 to seats near the Attention locus is a VS-B deferred item owned by Attention and Co-op because it needs a per-seat locus-distance or locus-body read Attention does not publish (preferred direction: seats on the locus body only; OQ-SN-2).

**SN-6. Forbidden inputs (HARD CONSTRAINT).**
*Rule.* The following never appear in any exposure or recovery term, in any mutator, or in any condition that changes `level`: **darkness in any form** (an unlit seat, an unlit room, the lamp off, the beam off, "alone in the dark"; EM:95 principle 8, EM:1117 OQ-4, LO-31); **Suit vitals** (hypoxia, cold, reserve; SE-27 bans a hypoxia hallucination); **the player's own noise** (it is Attention's input, so counting it here would double count); **any `PERCEPTION_OUT` value**; **any Perception, Lighting or Audio value**; and **player action counts or any verb or item that lowers sanity** (no self-harm lever). A source-scan lint of the D.1 level path (every symbol that can reach `level`, `pending`, or an exposure or recovery term) for these symbols, and one AC for each, are written in Acceptance Criteria. The lint scope is the level path only: the `lit` condition of SN-16 is an onset condition, not a level term.
*Why.* Pillar 3: going dark must stay a pure choice with no hidden price. Pillar 1: exposure is the web, not a stat the player manages by a side channel. The creative-director's constraint on this GDD is "that GDD must not make darkness (an unlit seat, an unlit room) a source of sanity loss, and must adopt or counter this text when it lands": it is adopted here.
*Edge notes.* The recovery term reads Graph compartment bands, not Suit vitals, and carries no light, power or lamp condition (SN-8). Any "you are alone in the dark" cue elsewhere is routed to Sanity as a non-draining cue and never a drain.

**SN-7. Attention coupling: yes, one-way, a rate by phase and tier.**
*Rule.* Sanity reads `attention.view` fields `phase`, `tier`, `tell_outcome` and `tell_epoch`, and does **not** read `level_pm` (it is presentation-quantised, AT-26, and reading it would leak the hidden level). E2 per run: CALM 0; TELL `EXP_TELL[tier]` with tier 1 to 3 = 6 / 12 / 20 (tier 0 = 0); BREACH `EXP_BREACH` 28; SUBSIDING `EXP_SUBSIDING` 14. On the run where `tell_epoch` differs from the stored `last_tell_epoch` and `tell_outcome` is BREACHED, add `EXP_BREACH_ENTRY` 1500 mSU to `pending` (capped, SN-9) and store the epoch, so a restore never double-credits a startle. Sanity never writes Attention and feeds nothing into its thresholds (ATT:374). The tell is a truth channel: **no phantom tell and no false calm at any tier or stage** (AT-17, permanent, closed at the gate by pillar).
*Why.* This answers ATT:374: "it states whether Attention events change sanity and by how much (Sanity's rule)". The loop Attention to sanity to lies to player noise to Attention passes through the human, never through the simulation, and is documented as intended.
*Edge notes.* The reader notes of Attention's `tier` and `tell_outcome` do not yet list Sanity (`phase` already names Sanity and `tell_epoch` is read by 'all'), and ATT:374 names only `level_pm` and `phase`; adding Sanity to those reader notes is a routed request to Attention (C11), not a quotation of its contract.

**SN-8. Recovery: a settled home, an idle trickle, a saner crewmate.**
*Rule.* `recovery R = (settled ? REC_SETTLED : REC_IDLE) + (crew_saner ? REC_CREW : 0)` with `REC_SETTLED` 12, `REC_IDLE` 1, `REC_CREW` 3 mSU per run `[PLACEHOLDER]`. `settled` is true when the seat's body is the home body AND every atmosphere band (`band_o2`, `band_p`, `band_t` of `compartment(cid)`) of the seat's compartment is OK AND Attention `phase` is CALM AND `tier` is 0. `crew_saner` (VS-B, dormant) is true when another bound seat within `CREW_R` is at least one stage saner. No light, power or lamp condition appears anywhere in recovery. A falsification grants no recovery (SN-17). `REC_MAX` = `REC_SETTLED` + `REC_CREW` = 15.
*Why.* It gives the home and sortie rhythm (Pillar 5) and never rewards or punishes darkness. Compartment bands are a Graph read, not Suit vitals.
*Edge notes.* **Home-refuge invariant (build assertion):** `max(RING_EXPOSURE) < REC_SETTLED` (8 < 12), so a settled home compartment nets a gain in any ring. A home compartment with a band outside OK loses `settled` and recovers only `REC_IDLE`; this is Graph truth. A home-base recovery floor (`REC_HOME_BASE`) is **not adopted** (checkpoint CQ3, closes OQ-SN-9; reopened only on a playtest signal): a home body has `ABOARD_EXPOSURE` 0 and a home in ring 1 has E1 0, so net is at least 0 absent E2 and E3, deeper rings are bounded by the ambient floor (SN-29), and the cure for a damaged home is repairing one compartment to all-OK bands, which is Pillar 5's ship cost and a Pillar 1 loop.

**SN-9. One lowering path, the net cap and the pending pool.**
*Rule.* Only D.1 lowers `level` (the debug `force_level` and `force_stage` are the only exceptions; they live in the dev-only source root (`overlay_source_root`, `src/debug/`), registered only when the `dev` feature tag is present (`overlay_build_gate`), SN-28). Net loss per run is `clamp(E - R + draw, -REC_MAX, +DRIFT_MAX_PER_RUN)` with `DRIFT_MAX_PER_RUN` 36 mSU per run (180 per second) `[PLACEHOLDER]`; the lower clamp is unreachable by construction (recovery is at most 15 and exposure is at least 0) and is kept as a build assertion a retune must not break. Startle and every future one-shot enter `pending` and drain only into the headroom left under the cap; the drain floor is 0. `PENDING_MAX` 3000 mSU `[PLACEHOLDER]`; an addition beyond it is discarded (overflow only ever reduces drift, the safe direction). *(Orchestrator ruling: SD proposed 4500, GD 3000; 3000 is adopted, two stacked startles.)*
*Why.* If startle bypassed the cap, a BREACH plus a horror in the beam could drop 55000 mSU in under a minute and unlock lethal friendly harm without minutes of lies, breaking the concept's "crossing the threshold must be slow and costly". Routing every pulse through the cap makes the lead invariants of SN-10 true build assertions rather than hopes.
*Edge notes.* The rulings' "about 42 runs" to drain a startle (1500 / 36) holds only with zero other exposure. With exposure present the headroom is smaller: ring 1 aboard in BREACH (`E` = 20 + 28 = 48, `R` 1) is capped at 36 with headroom 0, so the startle waits until BREACH ends; SUBSIDING at home in ring 1 (`E` 14, `R` 1, because the phase is not CALM) leaves headroom 23. The cap and the invariants are unaffected; only that prose number changed. A debt that outlives the danger ("the hangover") is the intended feel, bounded by `PENDING_MAX`.

#### Lead invariants, the harm gate and the sinks

**SN-10. Lead invariants: the sanity twin of the fairness gate.**
*Rule.* Four build assertions (D.4 (i) to (iv); (iv) adds the 150-run dwell to bound (ii)), evaluated from the knob table at boot and re-run on every retune (formula `drift_lead_invariant`, D.4): (i) from stage-1 entry (70000) to `SAN_TELL_ON` (30000), at least `SAN_STAGE1_TO_TELL_MIN_S` 220 s at the worst-case cap: 40000 / 36 = 1112 runs = 222.4 s; (ii) from `SAN_TELL_ON` to `SAN_HARM_ENTER` (15000), at least `SAN_TELL_LEAD_S` 60 s: 15000 / 36 = 417 runs = 83.4 s; (iii) from stage-1 entry to `SAN_HARM_ENTER`, at least `SAN_MIN_ONSET_TO_HARM_S` 300 s: 55000 / 36 = 1528 runs = 305.6 s, and the bit sets on the 150th consecutive run below `SAN_HARM_ENTER` (SN-11), run 1677 = 335.4 s. Corollary assertions: `harm_eligible` implies `stage == 3` and implies `tell_on` (outside debug force); `max(RING_EXPOSURE) < REC_SETTLED`; the ambient-floor invariants (SN-29). The GDD must show the **cap is the binding bound**: the worst-case uncapped exposure at ring 3 aboard in BREACH with a horror near is `8 + 20 + 28 + 20 = 76` (`RING_EXPOSURE[3]` 8, `ABOARD_EXPOSURE` 20, `EXP_BREACH` 28, `EXP_HORROR` 20) against `R` = 1, far above 36, so `net` is 36 and the startle waits in `pending`; even without the horror term `8 + 20 + 28 = 56` exceeds the cap, and ring 1 aboard in BREACH alone (`0 + 20 + 28 = 48`) exceeds it.
*Why.* It is the sanity analogue of `W_MIN_WARNING_S` (LSG:144). A friendly-harm gate that could open inside minutes would make the guard rails (system gate, victim tell, web attribution) decorative. The concept asks that "crossing the threshold must be slow and costly".
*Edge notes.* The margins are thin: 2.4 s on (i) and 5.6 s on (iii); 36 is the largest cap that passes (37 gives 1487 runs = 297.4 s and fails (iii)). **Any retune of a threshold, the cap, the divisor or an exposure rate re-runs all four**; reflexion P-7 (an invariant written against "the default" must be re-checked whenever a later decision adds reachable states) applies to every Tuning Knob.

**SN-11. The harm gate and the victim tell: one SIM bit.**
*Rule.* `harm_eligible` sets after `SAN_HARM_DWELL_RUNS` 150 (30 s) consecutive runs with `level < SAN_HARM_ENTER` 15000 and clears **at once** on any run with `level > SAN_HARM_EXIT` 20000; a run with `level >= SAN_HARM_ENTER` resets the dwell counter. `tell_on` sets on `level < SAN_TELL_ON` 30000 and clears on `level > SAN_TELL_OFF` 35000, with no dwell (all `[PLACEHOLDER]`). Interaction's query is ratified verbatim: **`actor_sanity_below_harm_threshold(player_id) -> bool`** returns `sanity.state.harm_eligible` for that PlayerId. It is a pure function of SIM data, never of `PERCEPTION_OUT` (IA-31 gate step 4; IA:454). A SIM read view `sanity_view(player_id) -> {stage, tell_on, harm_eligible}` (summary class, no raw level) serves the Filter, Adaptive Audio & Tells and the Debug Overlay. **The class-(c) render level IS that bit**: the Filter reads `harm_eligible` from SIM and no `PERCEPTION_OUT` copy of it exists. This records IA-31 precondition (1) (AC-IA-126 half a). Half (b), that every real HORROR subject carries a `repel` declaration with the same `verb_name_key`, tool, prompt conditions, duration class, bar length, noise class and cue set, is Horror's: Horror's draft does not yet record it *(provisional — Horror Entities In Design, draft on disk, horror-entities.md)*: HE-39 declares only the dormant suppressant interface `repel(origin, T)` (a different thing), and HE-38 states that HORROR takes no verb at MVP and reserves the VS-B `repel` declaration fields (IA:1277) by name in its Open Questions only. **VS-B `begin_harm` therefore stays BLOCKED** until Horror's GDD records the plate-parity declaration; Horror acknowledges it as owed at Vertical Slice B (not MVP), and the precondition blocks any `begin_harm` story. The bit is per actor; Interaction separately requires a target of a different PlayerId. **Conditional second reader (VS-B):** if Modular Ship Assembly adopts `CUT_STRANDS` (MSA-18, ASK F2), Assembly's `validate` may read the cutter's bit through the same query `actor_sanity_below_harm_threshold`. It is a pure SIM read, the same bit, never a PERCEPTION_OUT copy, and a stranding it permits is recorded through `record_harm_event` attributed to the web. No other simulation reader is permitted.
*Why.* The IA:270 and IA:454 leak argument: any render level different from the gate bit lets a plate on a "horror" tell it from a real horror at zero cost. The victim tell precedes eligibility by at least `SAN_TELL_LEAD_S` through SN-10 (Pillar 2 in spirit).
*Edge notes.* A mid-channel flip is Interaction's `ABORT(NOT_ELIGIBLE)` at the next `LIT_RECHECK_TICKS` check (AC-IA-88). The hysteresis band (5000 mSU) makes it rare, not impossible. A harm channel is `HARM_CHANNEL_TICKS` 120 ticks = 10 runs, so the level moves by at most 10 x 36 = 360 mSU during one. A flip therefore needs the actor to start the channel within 10 x `REC_MAX` = 150 mSU below `SAN_HARM_EXIT` while recovering (an actor leaving eligibility at a settled home); it cannot arise from drift at the entry edge. *(Correction: the game-designer's claim that a natural flip is impossible holds only for the entry edge; QA should test the exit edge.)* In the MVP (single seat) the bit is computed and queryable but no target exists; class (c) rendering is disabled by `CLASSES_ENABLED` and exercised with stub actors.

**SN-12. Lethality and the revert switch: one switch, one owner.**
*Rule.* Sanity records the user decision verbatim ("This is like project zomboid, death is a bitch. It happens."): a completed act (every step landed, `HARM_TOTAL_BP` 10000 >= `SUIT_HP_FULL` 10000) is lethal through Suit; when and how death arrives is Suit's (SE-23, SE-24; IA-31). `SUIT_EXO_BYPASS_LIMITER` is **1** in the registry (Suit CK-2). The down-only fallback is that single Suit knob, changed by the creative-director on the IDX:280 playtest signal; Sanity adds no second switch, and this GDD never restates the old pre-decision value as a default.
*Why.* One switch, one owner (the registry's exception note). The index's high-risk entry reads: "Lethal friendly harm and verification cost must feel fair; no unfalsifiable lies. Playtest signal: friendly-fire deaths told as stories and crews keep playing together; revert to down-only if not."
*Edge notes.* Conflict C3 routed to Interaction: IA:270, the IA:891 knob row and IA:1345 OQ 3 still describe the pre-decision state of that knob; their refresh is owed.

**SN-13. The harm-event sink and web attribution.**
*Rule.* `record_harm_event(actor, victim, tick)` is a **Class B** mutator (clamp-never-reject) that Interaction calls inside its apply WriteScope at channel completion (sim systems never subscribe, CR-10; the apply is infallible, CR-9). It appends `{tick, actor, victim, attributed_to: WEB}` to the `incidents` ring (`INCIDENTS_MAX` 16 `[PLACEHOLDER]`, oldest dropped) and changes **nothing else**: no level change for the actor, no reward, no recovery, no E4 (a witness's E4 is VS-B). Scars, logs and consequences belong to Sanity Scars, the Codex and the Failure Contract, which read the ring.
*Why.* IA-31 "no reward path"; attribution to the web keeps friendly harm a story the web told and not an intentional PvP loop.
*Edge notes.* Conflict C10 routed to Interaction: IA-31 says it "emits one `IA_HARM_EVENT`"; the legal form is a call to this mutator, a wording refresh and not a rename. **Class B robustness (clamp-never-reject, CR-9; also `record_falsification`, SN-17):** a call with an unknown PlayerId or actor, an unresolvable `subject_ref` or an unknown `check_kind` is a no-op with exactly one log line, never a FAULTED mutation and never an exception (QA-12).

**SN-14. Self-knowledge: Sanity creates no new self-sign.**
*Rule.* The one-bit prompt (the creative-director's self-knowledge ruling, IA:270 and IA-39: a prompt or an immediate `NOT_ELIGIBLE` is "a one-bit statement about the actor's own sanity relative to the harm threshold. That is honest self-knowledge, which Pillar 4 allows") is the only hard self-knowledge. The diegetic sign is the stage leaks the actor already sees in the world: the stage-1 gleam on instruments, the stage-2 fringe at the cone rim, the stage-3 hue bleed, and, at eligibility, crewmates drawn as horrors. Nothing on the actor's body or HUD (ART 5.3: "there is no self-tell"; ART 7.0).
*Why.* It answers IA OQ 29 (a recommended, non-binding diegetic sign) as "the leak at the edge of the light is the sign", without a body sign or a meter.
*Edge notes.* Conflict C2 closes as "satisfied by existing leaks". The concept's P4(c) phrase "the attacker's palette leak or a visible sanity sign" describes the **victim-side** fringe (the attacker's leak seen by others, ART 5.5), not a self-sign.

#### Lies: eligibility, class (b), falsification, the ladder

**SN-15. Lie eligibility: the guard rails every lie of every class passes.**
*Rule.* A lie is created only if **all** of the following hold at activation; L-C, L-W, L-A and L-G are re-checked on every later run, L-S against the lie's stored `onset_stage`, and L-2I only for the loss of a fixed gauge it relied on (both below); a failing re-check INVALIDATES the lie (SN-16).
- **L-C, channel.** The target is a module-body-channel surface (a module's sprite, gauge, inspect card or WORN descriptors; LSG LS-14, LSG:864) or, VS-A, a decoy overlay. **Never** any consequence-channel item: trail VFX (mist, drip, frost, scorch, debris, puddle), the `acoustic` classes at a descriptor's anchor, Suit body cues, lamp brightness from `served_pm`, the `AIRFLOW` draught, the `SEAL_STRAIN` strain; nor trails (FT-2), geometry (LY-34, MSA F1), `body.state` or positions (BDY-30), anchors (ART:68-77), the human channel (voice, a crewmate's beam), Attention outputs (AT-17), prompts or durations (IA-10 to IA-13), save or pause surfaces (SL H6). **Never the seam lane either** (MSA F1, MSA-23 fallback): the sprites, glyphs and inspect cards of a welded seam's own PIPE and DUCT valves and its CABLE breaker are not lie targets until the Graph (ASK G8) and Failure & Trail (ASK FT1) publish a consequence-channel lane witness that exists at zero gradient; MSA-35 truth tags (bead, cap plate, socket geometry, `CUT_BLOCKED`, install prompts) are never wrapped. **Never a gouge footprint**: a WORN descriptor whose cause is a real horror's GOUGE (Horror HE-32 footprint, FT:72 caveat) is never a lie target for any seat while that entity is registered. While any seat has a HIDE on an entity, no lie of any class may target a surface the entity has gouged, so the footprint survives on ducts and cables as well as on the hull. **Sanity may not add either**: a false-broken sprite adds no scorch, no sparks-as-arc-POP, no mist, no trail.
- **L-W, witness.** A FALSE_OK lie (degraded or failed shown healthy) requires a currently active consequence-channel witness for the fault, active in the **fault's compartment** (the compartment holding the node the lying surface reports on), and that compartment must lie within `WITNESS_HOPS_MAX` 2 `[PLACEHOLDER]` compartment hops of the lying surface's compartment over the Graph's `neighbors(cid)` (LS-19); the same compartment is hops 0. A FALSE_FAIL lie (healthy shown broken) requires that the fault compartment carry no descriptor for the claimed failure AND at least one positive honest cue of health there (`served_pm` lamp brightness, flow, the absence of the claimed trail), under the same bound. **Absence alone does not qualify.** *(Checkpoint CQ1, closes OQ-SN-15: Player Fantasy target 2 is "walk to the thing the gauge is about"; a same-compartment witness would make every remote gauge un-lieable and empty the lie set on a five-compartment home; a hop count is computable from a published read, a seconds bound is not. L-G and L-S still apply, so a remote FALSE_OK never extends a clock the body is already counting.)*
- **L-2I, a second instrument survives.** For one seat, no loop may have every instrument lying at once. The honest second instrument for a loop is one of: a fixed gauge on the same loop in another compartment; `suit.o2` (the occupant compartment's `p_o2`, Suit VA-16) or `suit.warmth` (that compartment's T and P, Suit VA-19; credited for the COOLANT loop's thermal quantity only, QA-3), each valid **only for the seat's current compartment**; or the scanner (`tool_scanner`, honest in the MVP, SN-19), credited **only if the seat carries it and can equip it** (a seat without the scanner has no honest second instrument on a single-instrument loop, so a lie on that loop is ineligible for that seat, QA-2). `suit.integrity`, `suit.reserve` and `suit.charge` measure the suit, not a ship loop, and never count. For POWER, FUEL, COOLANT flow and INTEGRITY only another fixed gauge on the loop or the scanner counts. In the VS-A slice where readings or the scanner may lie, at least one honest instrument must remain per loop per seat (`SECOND_INSTRUMENT_MIN` 1). **L-2I is evaluated at activation.** After activation it INVALIDATES the lie only when a fixed gauge it relied on is destroyed or lost; it is never re-evaluated on seat movement, on a dropped scanner (the scanner stays in the world) or on a door crossing, because a lie that vanished when the seat walked through a door would be an uncaused-vanish tell (QA-2). *(Orchestrator ruling: the rulings' "a gauge on the same loop elsewhere" is unsatisfiable for the slice's single-instrument loops, one fuel tank sight-glass and one reservoir, pump and radiator set; R23's own words "solo MVP needs rung 2 that does not need a walk" are the warrant for counting the scanner and the two loop-quantity Suit readings; checkpoint K2 removed the three Suit keys that measure the suit.)*
- **L-L, leak.** Every lie carries a non-empty `leak_modes` bitmask. HR-5's tri-modal tell (violet gleam, GLIDE motion signature, sound signature) is the default and the **first lie** carries all three; every lie entry's `leak_modes` contains the GLEAM bit (the palette mode) and the motion bit (GLIDE); the Filter may drop only SOUND, at stages 2 and 3 for plausibility, never below two, so a player with weak audio or weak colour discrimination always retains one (Perception Filter PF-15, PF-G8, C-7). "Subtle is allowed; absent is not" (ART:73). Decoy lies carry the violet rim. A HIDE entry (class a; stage 3 only) carries no per-entity outline, because an outline around empty space would mark the position of a hidden thing: its leak is the standing stage-3 world leak (AD-OQ-2).
- **L-I, immunity.** A subject checked by `field_check` (or truly repaired or inspected through the full channel, SN-17) is immune for that seat for `DEBUNK_IMMUNE_S` 60 s `[PLACEHOLDER]` (safe range 45 to 120, because the build assertion `DEBUNK_IMMUNE_S >= max(LIE_GAP_S)`, 60 >= 45, refuses anything below the longest gap; the window is exclusive of its end run), whether or not it was lying, so uniform immunity leaks nothing.
- **L-A, no alarm class.** A lie never uses alarm-class presentation (the Alarm Red sweep, an alarm sound class); `alarm_eligible` is Graph-owned true state and "alarm class never first" (LSG:563, LSG:1153). A lie never suppresses a true alarm.
- **L-S, severity ceiling for FALSE_OK.** Evaluated at activation and on every re-check against the lie's stored `onset_stage`, never the current stage (a stage drop never ends a lie, SN-16); a lie is INVALIDATED only if the subject's true severity worsens beyond what `onset_stage` allows (QA-1). At onset stage 1 a FALSE_OK lie may hide only a subject whose `hp` is DEGRADED (`HP_FAIL` 3000 < hp < `HP_OK` 7000) and whose active forced mode is not in the data list `lethal_fault_modes`; at stage 2 it may hide DEGRADED or FAILED; at stage 3, any module-body fault with an active witness. A subject whose true fault is on the `lethal_fault_modes` list (in the MVP slice the O2 seam leak after a weld and hull fatigue to breach) takes FALSE_OK only at stage `LETHAL_FAULT_MIN_STAGE` 2 or deeper `[PLACEHOLDER]`. *(Orchestrator ruling: adopted from the game-designer's GD-12 and GD-17; the Graph publishes no `lethal_clock_s` read, so the data list is the proxy, OQ-SN-10.)*
- **L-G, atmosphere guard.** No FALSE_OK lie may be live on a loop while the seat's current compartment atmosphere band for that loop's quantity is outside OK; the lie is INVALIDATED at the next run. A lie must never extend a lethal clock the Suit's body cues are already counting. It is the sanity-side companion of `W_MIN_WARNING_S` 10 s.
*Why.* P4 "no lie is unfalsifiable; at least one channel of truth always remains" and P1's closed lists. Each rail answers a named failure: L-C and L-A the consequence channel; L-W and L-2I the cost rule (a lie with nothing to check it against is unfalsifiable); L-L the palette; L-I the sweep exploit; L-S and L-G the cheap death.
*Edge notes.* Proposed but **not adopted**: L-F, a cost floor `VERIFY_FLOOR_S` 1.5 s so that a lie whose witness sits beside its gauge (falsifiable at zero cost) is ineligible (OQ-SN-5). A gauge that carries no remote, magnitude or trend information that a local witness lacks is decoration and a lie on it is a non-event; that is a content gate routed to In-World Instruments and Derelict Authoring (OQ-SN-11), not a Sanity mutator. A surface that is another lie's witness is never itself a lying surface (a witness is a consequence-channel item).

**SN-16. Class (b), false system state: kinds, reach, onset, lifecycle.** *(MVP-live.)*
*Rule.*
- **Kinds** (registry: `lie_kinds`). FALSE_OK (degraded or failed shown healthy); FALSE_FAIL (healthy shown broken); GLIDE (a reading drifts toward the comfort side if the true state is degraded or failed, toward the alarm side if healthy); SPRITE_SWAP (a rotor spinning on a stopped machine, or a housing shown cracked on a whole one). SPRITE_SWAP uses only the machine's own honest state frames, so it is eligible only for a module whose sheet carries both frames of the pair (running and stopped; whole and cracked), a content gate routed to In-World Instruments and Derelict Authoring. The kind is restricted by true state and L-W (SN-15). Per-stage weights `KIND_W[kind][stage]` `[PLACEHOLDER]`, comfort kinds (FALSE_OK, GLIDE) weighted above fright kinds (FALSE_FAIL, SPRITE_SWAP) at stages 1 and 2, fright kinds rising at stage 3, SPRITE_SWAP only where the reach allows: stage 1 = 45 / 25 / 30 / 0; stage 2 = 40 / 25 / 30 / 5 (SPRITE_SWAP of a glow sprite only); stage 3 = 25 / 30 / 15 / 30. If the stage-3 machine-frame sets are cut (Instruments IW-34), `has_both_frames` removes SPRITE_SWAP eligibility and the stage-3 weights are renormalised over the remaining legal kinds (D.5's weighted pick sums only legal kinds). *(Orchestrator ruling: three proposals existed, the creative-director's comfort-versus-fright intent, a flat 40/30/20/10, and per-stage tuples; the per-stage table satisfies the first.)*
- **Reach by stage (the intensity ladder).** Stage 1: gauge needle, readout (the card numeral, the `displayed_value` of the needle ref's `Perceived`; TICK and STALE are modes of that numeral only, Instruments C2) and the inspect card (the card is the same instrument as its wall gauge and lies exactly when the gauge lies, ART 7.0 and HR-14; a card that stayed honest while its gauge lied would make inspecting a free falsifier, the failure KRN:96's design test names; AD-C1). Stage 2: plus state glyph and the lamp-housing glow sprite (the only route by which a "light" lie exists in the MVP; the real lamp brightness stays true). Stage 3: plus the whole machine sprite ("the lie extends from the gauge to the sprite"). Intensity scales by count (`LIES_MAX[stage]` 1 / 2 / 4), kind and reach, **never** by removing a leak or touching a witness.
- **Lit-entry event (the only onset trigger).** The lookup chain uses real queries: `PlayerId` -> actor (the kernel binding table) -> Body `actor_section(actor_id)` and `actor_local_px(actor_id)` (BDY-29) -> Layout `room_at(section_id, px)` (LY:296) -> `compartment_of_room(room_id)` (LY:297), or Graph `compartment_at(room_ref)` (LS-19) -> Graph `nodes_in(cid)` -> Emitter `lit(ref)` (EM-25). Every query exists on disk; the one remaining named assumption is the mapping from a Graph node id to the OBJECT subject ref that `lit(ref)` answers for (who registers a gauge as a subject), routed to Interaction and Derelict Authoring *(provisional)*. For each seat P the **candidate set** is `C(P) = nodes_in(cid(P)) ∩ {lit}`, restricted to lie-eligible module-body subjects, and the previous run's set is stored as `lit_prev`. An **event** for P is a subject S entering `C(P)` between two runs (in `C(P)` now, not in `lit_prev`) AND at least one holds: **(a) a compartment-entry batch**: when P's compartment changes, `entry_pending` is set for P and stays set until P leaves that compartment or the batch fires; on the first run at which `C(P)` is non-empty, `entry_pending` clears and every member of `C(P)` enters together; **or (b) a non-own-beam cause**: S entered `C(P)` for a cause other than P's own chest lamp (power came on, a fixture relit), told apart through the static lit layer. Emitter State is **requested** to publish `lit_static(ref)`, the existing EM-23 static-layer cache (fixtures, lanterns, fault glow, resting deployables) *(provisional — a routed request; `lit_by(point)` is a budgeted probe, `MAX_PROBES_PER_TICK` 4, returning UNKNOWN, reserved for Horror, and Sanity does not use it)*. Crewmate-beam causes are VS-B; with no `lit_static`, only (a) fires. A subject already lit by a fixture when P enters the compartment is in `C(P)` on the first run there and enters through (a), so the event is defined over set membership and never over a not-lit-to-lit transition. A change in S's `lit` caused only by P turning, facing or toggling their own chest lamp inside the same compartment is **not** an event. Each subject has a per-seat debounce `LIT_ENTRY_DEBOUNCE_S` 20 s `[PLACEHOLDER]` (a no-draw gate: an event less than 100 runs after the last event on that subject for that seat that passed the debounce is dropped without a draw and does not refresh the stamp; an event at exactly 100 runs passes). A compartment where nothing is lit has an empty `C(P)` and produces **zero** onsets. *(Orchestrator ruling: this defines R16's term; the chest lamp is per-facing and sweeps subjects in and out of the beam continuously, so a bare edge detector would spam the draw. `entry_pending` persists until the seat leaves because a timed variant lets a player stay unlit for a few seconds, then light up, and receive no lies, a free dodge.)*
- **Pre-draw gates.** Before any draw: class (b) enabled; stage at least 1; active plus PENDING lies for the seat below `LIES_MAX[stage]`; at least `LIE_GAP_S[stage]` (45 / 25 / 10 s) since the seat's last onset; the subject passes SN-15; the subject is not immune; the subject has no live (PENDING or ACTIVE) lie for this seat (**at most one live lie per (seat, `subject_ref`)**; the inspect card is a surface of that same lie, never a second one; so the Filter never has a collision to resolve; Perception Filter OQ-PF-16). An event that fails a gate performs **no** draw and advances no counter (the lie stream is `"sanity.lie/<PlayerId>/<stable_id>"`, one counter per subject counting only that seat's gated events on that subject, kept in a map in `sanity.ledger` and pruned on subject destruction), so the cap cannot be used to burn draws and an unrelated lit-entry elsewhere cannot shift this subject's dice. Events in one run are processed in the order of `rng_derive(world_seed, "sanity.lie.order/<p>/<stable_id>", the subject's visit counter)` ascending, a pure function that advances no counter, so low subject ids have no standing preference.
- **Onset.** D.5: a gated event accepts with probability `ONSET_PM[stage]` per mille (150 / 300 / 550 `[PLACEHOLDER]`), doubled for a subject repaired within `REPAIR_BIAS_S` 60 s ("it came undone"); on accept it draws kind, life (`LIE_LIFE_S` 40 to 120 s via `rng_int`) and activation delay (`ONSET_DELAY_RUNS` 10 to 25), and stores `onset_stage`, the stage at the accept. **Lie timing:** `start_run` is the accept run; `active_run = start_run + delay_runs` (published in the perception entry; Perception Filter OQ-PF-17); `end_run = active_run + life_runs`, so the life is counted **from activation** and is the whole ACTIVE span (the Filter's D.2 `L_s` holds as written). `first_lie` is true on the entry accepted while `lies_seen` is 0 and is stored with it, so the Filter knows which entry keeps all three leak modes.
- **Lifecycle.** PENDING (accepted, invisible for the delay so the activation can re-validate SN-15) to ACTIVE (in `sanity.perception`) to one of EXPIRED (`end_run` reached: the time rung), DEBUNKED (`record_falsification` or a full-channel reveal, SN-17), INVALIDATED (a guard rail no longer holds, the subject is destroyed, the true state now equals the displayed state, or the class is disabled by data). All three end types leave `sanity.perception` on the same run and none is announced: the Filter returns the displayed value to the truth on the glide path at its `GLIDE_RETURN_RATE` (registry; never a snap, never a settle) with the leak amplitude following the remaining displayed-minus-true discrepancy, identically for EXPIRED, INVALIDATED and DEBUNKED, so the end type is never an oracle (ART 7.3, SNV-11, AD-C2). The lie's clock runs in sim time: if the seat leaves the compartment the clock keeps running and an unexpired lie is drawn again on return from its remaining life; a witness that ends before `end_run` (the fault was repaired) collapses the lie at the next run. **A stage change never ends a lie**: existing lies run to expiry and only new onsets use the new stage's caps and reach (a lie that vanishes exactly at a stage boundary is a tell of the boundary and uncaused pixel motion). *(Orchestrator ruling: SD proposed invalidating on a stage drop, GD keeping; keeping is adopted.)*
- **The first lie.** `lies_seen` (ledger, WORLD) starts at 0. The first lie ever raised in a world is FALSE_FAIL or GLIDE, on a subject whose loop has no fault and no forced mode, with all three leak modes. It costs the player one `field_check` (`FIELD_CHECK_TICKS` 120 = 2 s) plus `T_EQUIP` plus reach (D.7) and teaches the ladder before a costly lie can land. *(Orchestrator ruling: adopted from GD-14 on competence grounds; if no fault-free lit subject exists the first-lie onset is deferred, nothing is raised and `lies_seen` does not advance until one does, because a costly first lie would defeat the purpose, QA-13.)*
*Why.* Lies meet the player where they look. The lit-entry rule keeps every lie inside the seat's light (a compartment where nothing is lit yields no lies, and a seat that keeps its own lamp off there gives up the lit-only verbs and the readings: a trade, not an exploit) and keeps the draw counter a function of the player's own actions (SN-24).
*Edge notes.* All lies are per seat: two seats at different stages see their own view of one gauge, which is what makes rung 3 work. A subject eligible for both FALSE_OK and FALSE_FAIL takes its kind from its true state. If a lit-entry batch holds more candidates than the cap allows, draws stop at the cap and the remainder advance nothing. -> ADR candidate: the candidate-set data format, a Sanity-owned `assets/data/sanity/lie_targets.json` mapping module kind to surface kinds to witness descriptor modes and the `lethal_fault_modes` list; no lie is encoded in any authored template (DA-37).

**SN-17. The falsification sink, the room rung and the reveal price.**
