*Rule.* **`record_falsification(player_id, check_kind, subject_ref, outcome)`** (the signature ratified verbatim from IA-30) is a **Class B** mutator called from Interaction's infallible `field_check` apply. `outcome` is the channel result Interaction knows (COMPLETED); **Sanity derives whether a lie existed from its own ledger and never from the argument** (C5, a wording note to Interaction). Effect: any ACTIVE or PENDING lie on `subject_ref` for `player_id` becomes DEBUNKED (`debunked_run` stamped), L-I immunity starts for `DEBUNK_IMMUNE_S`, and the **level is unchanged** (no grounding gain, and no penalty for a check on a non-lie). The same effect applies when a repair or inspect channel completes on the subject through the full channel: that completion collapses a FALSE_FAIL lie as a "room" rung and delivers its true reason only as the ordinary outcome after the full channel (IA-10 to IA-13), never instantly. *(Routed request to Interaction: Interaction calls the same mutator, with a repair or inspect `check_kind`, at the completion of those channels. Without the call Sanity cannot learn of a repair that changes nothing in the Graph. A repair of a truly degraded subject is also seen as a Graph `hp` rise (a rise of any amount at a repair completion or from any Graph repair mutator), which stamps `repaired_recent[]` with the run, and ends a FALSE_OK lie by the witness ending; OQ-SN-12.)*
*Reveal price (KRN:96, KRN:202).* "Sanity prices how a reason reaches a hallucinating player": a reason code is computed from true state and the kernel never filters it; the price is paid in channel time, light and presence. Durations are identical for hallucinated-broken, truly broken and destroyed subjects (IA-12, IA-13), no PLAYER `validate` reads module-body `hp`, `state`, `forced_mode` or `served_pm` (IA P4 lint), and a rejection must not let the player falsify a hallucination at zero time, risk or trust cost. Kernel OQ 14 (commands aimed at phantoms) is CLOSED by IA-13 and is not reopened: phantoms have no subject, are never `lit`, never take focus, and nothing is submitted or rejected.
*Why.* Sanity stays a pure function of exposure and recovery (no meta-farming, no "reward path" smell); the ladder's payoff is certainty (the P4 cost rule). The scanner result is never stored as truth about a lie in `interaction.state` (IA-30).
*Edge notes.* A `field_check` on a subject with no lie stamps immunity, changes nothing visible, and leaves the level unchanged. If a check completes on the same tick as the lie's `end_run`, expiry wins first (P3 step) and the check then records immunity on a non-lying subject. A debunked lie stops lying through the same glide-return as any other end, but the immunity window is uniform, so the player cannot infer from its presence whether a subject lied.

**SN-18. The verification ladder.**
*Rule.* Rungs, in the Graph's order (LSG:44): (1) **the room itself**; (2) **a second instrument elsewhere on the ship**; (3) **a saner crewmate** (VS-B); (4) **time**. Every lie has a stated finite cost on at least two rungs, guaranteed by L-W (rung 1) and L-2I (rung 2). The time rung is the lie's remaining life, at most `LIE_LIFE_S` max (120 s `[PLACEHOLDER]`), and its price is the real fault bleeding the room meanwhile, so the world cost of waiting out the maximum life must be non-trivial for every MVP fault (an AC in Acceptance Criteria). Cost columns carried by formula D.7: time (s), lit required, noise class, tool swap, reach, trust. A saner crewmate costs trust: believing a friend's word over one's own eyes. A crewmate is drawn as a horror only to an actor whose `harm_eligible` bit is set (SN-20), and the human channel (voice, beam) is never altered, so a saner crewmate's voice is always a true instrument. The `field_check` is the shortcut that turns a rung-1 or rung-2 observation into a cleared ledger entry at `FIELD_CHECK_TICKS` cost. The break-even between walking to the witness and scanning from where one stands is `d* = (FIELD_CHECK_TICKS / TICK_RATE) x (V_WALK x TICK_RATE / TILE_PX)` = 2 s x (2 x 60 / 48) = 2 s x 2.5 tiles/s = 5 tiles; a witness nearer than 5 tiles is cheaper to look at, farther is cheaper to scan.
*Why.* "Checking costs time, risk or trust" (P4): each rung prices a different currency, so no single habit (scan everything, trust the gauge, wait) dominates. The immunity window is uniform and short enough that a sweep of every surface on entry is not dominant at two or fewer eligible surfaces (`DEBUNK_IMMUNE_S` 60, safe range 45 to 120; OQ-SN-7 closed).
*Edge notes.* The equip swap to scanner slot 3 has no registered duration (SE-11 registers none), so every scan cost in D.7 is a **lower bound** (`T_EQUIP` carried as 0 until Suit or the Controller registers one, OQ-SN-6). `field_check` has no declared noise class yet; a silent stationary scan costs no Attention, so Sanity requests a class at least walk-equivalent from Interaction (OQ-SN-13).

**SN-19. The scanner, the Suit readings switch and the rest of the instruments.**
*Rule.* In the MVP the scanner (`tool_scanner`, slot 3, `charges_max` 0, SE-11) is an **anchor**: its reading is honest, unwrapped, a reading in the reading register, never a verdict icon, never a real-or-fake binary, never Alarm Red or safe green (IA:972, F-3). In VS-A it may be lie-capable at stage 3 only, behind `SCANNER_LIE_STAGE` (default never), with the HR-5 tri-modal tell. Its price is `FIELD_CHECK_TICKS` 120 (Interaction's, cited and never re-valued) of lit channel plus the equip to slot 3 plus reach `REACH_PX` 72 plus Interaction's declared noise class. Sanity states a floor rationale `FIELD_CHECK_FLOOR_TICKS` 90 (a multiple of `GRAPH_STEP_TICKS` 6) below which a check is nearly free, routed to Interaction's knob note (REG:4919). All five Suit `true_value` keys (`suit.o2`, `suit.reserve`, `suit.integrity`, `suit.warmth`, `suit.charge`) are **honest in the MVP** (SE-29, locked). The VS-A per-key switch `READING_LIE_STAGE[key]` defaults to never; the designed order is `suit.warmth` first (the teaching beat: the numeral says warm, the breath says cold), then `suit.o2` and `suit.charge`, with `suit.integrity` and `suit.reserve` last. Body cues (breath, visor fog, rime, shiver, reserve valve click, load layer, seam jet, HEAVY back-frame, stagger, walk-only) are never corruptible (SE-25, SE-27). Rail counts (suppressant and glowstick) stay anchors (SE OQ-6); the ship-alarm and world-housing warning marks never lie (Graph `alarm_eligible` only); a suit-rung mark sits beside its reading and inherits that reading's class (HUD HS-30; the creative-director's recommendation, user confirmation pending, OQ-SN-36; at the MVP no reading lies, so every mark is honest). The flip of a key from honest to wrapped is the Filter's, on Sanity's switch. Suit never reads sanity; no Suit cue changes with sanity.
*Why.* Pillar 2 protects a first-time player's only death clock until the ladder is playtested (SE:103); the solo MVP needs a rung 2 that does not need a walk (R23).
*Edge notes.* The carried risk (SE:103) is that if VS-A never turns the switch on, the warmth teaching beat never ships; it is recorded as an Open Question (SE OQ-15). The "closed list" of Suit body cues quoted by the Graph (LSG:168, LSG:209, LSG:864) still lists "vignette"; Suit dropped it (SE-27, SE:132), so this GDD quotes Suit's current set (C7, a refresh owed to the Graph owner).

#### The other three classes (designed to the same depth; deferred, behind `CLASSES_ENABLED`)

**SN-20. Class (c), crewmates as horrors.** *(Deferred: blocked on Co-op, VS-B; exercised now with stub actors.)*
*Rule.* While actor A's `harm_eligible` bit is set, every other bound seat is drawn as a horror **for A only**, continuously, at the crewmate's **true position**, with the rig driven by the crewmate's **true velocity**, with the crewmate's **true beam** (shape, colour and chest-point origin) and **true voice** (ART 5.5 names beam shape, fringe and voice as three redundant tells, any one of which suffices; the beam and the voice are the human channel and are never altered). Victim side: A's `tell_on` drives the victim's tell (a violet fringe 2 px outside the contour and along both beam edges, breathing every 4 to 6 s, drawn in an unlit pass, casting no light, raising no Attention and silent; ART 5.5, HR-7), plus the honest P1 proximity cue from A's suit (physically real, played on every client; whether the source hears it is Adaptive Audio's). A hallucination alone harms nobody: harm still needs a deliberate lit `HARM_CHANNEL_TICKS` 120 channel with a cutter or welder (IA-31). The act is lethal and attributed to the web (SN-12, SN-13). Monsterisation never touches `body.state`, positions, prompts or durations. Ladder (D.7 row): rung 1 hear the voice and see the true beam colour; rung 2 a second look from another position *(a crewmate has no instrument, OQ-SN-8)*; rung 3 ask a saner crewmate; rung 4 leave eligibility (level above `SAN_HARM_EXIT`).
*Why.* It closes IA-31 precondition (1). Lead time from the victim's tell to eligibility is at least 83.4 s plus the 30 s dwell, 113.4 s, at the worst-case cap (SN-10).
*Edge notes.* **Pre-eligibility glimpses are rejected**: a crewmate flickering as a horror before the bit sets would reproduce the IA-31 leak (a horror shape without a plate means it is a crewmate); the preface to class (c) is carried by classes (a) and (b) and by the victim tell. An eligible actor stays eligible until `level > SAN_HARM_EXIT`: from the worst-case overshoot during the dwell (about 15000 - 150 x 36 = 9600) recovery to 20000 takes 10400 / 12 = 867 runs = 173 s in a settled home compartment, but 34.7 minutes at `REC_IDLE`; the group's practical cure is to bring the seat home. That is the intended co-op beat (escort) and belongs in Game Feel. Counterplay to the act itself is movement and voice: a victim who leaves reach within the channel survives (Interaction's `harm_step` accrues by reach).

**SN-21. Class (d), phantom salvage.** *(Deferred: VS-A, class (d); MVP ships none. Boarding & Salvage is designed.)*
*Rule.* Junk defs are real world scrap (IDB-22, ids 9000 to 9999, VS only), never per-player objects. Sanity dresses them per seat from a **Sanity-owned** lure-band file, `assets/data/sanity/lure_bands.json`, `{band_id, mid_g, class}`; the choice is deterministic from (instance id, seat, `sanity.lure` counter) via D.6, stored in `lures[]` (at most `LURES_MAX[stage]` = 0 / 2 / 3 per seat `[PLACEHOLDER]`, and never more than `LURE_FRACTION_MAX_PM` 400 per mille of the junk in the seat's lit set). A lure is eligible only if its pretended `weight_class` differs from the junk's true class AND the mass gap is at least `WEIGHT_TELL_MIN_PM` 300 (cited); otherwise the breath-step falsifier cannot work. **Real valuables are never dressed as junk** (anti-P5). A seat whose `cue_load` is HEAVY through stowed items gets no lure, because stowed HEAVY masks every carried class ("drop the plate first", SE OQ-8). More generally (Suit SE-28), `cue_load = max(carried, stowed)` with `STOWED_CUE_FLOOR_CLASS` MEDIUM, so the carried-weight field check is valid only while the stowed class is below the carried class under test. Cheap checks, cheapest first: (1) the **weight breath-step** (Interaction's VS-A `check_kind` HEFT, IA-30: pick it up; the Suit's load cue differs from the lure's class); (2) **SCAN** (`FIELD_CHECK_TICKS`); (3) **PALETTE** (VS-A: the violet leak on the sprite); (4) the **authored-absence read** (undisturbed dust, a missing verdict trail, DA:1176; a module's own fitting may carry a dried trail, a cheap field read); (5) the **trip home**, the dearest. Design test: the check costs at most 5 % of the trip home with the item (`C_check <= 0.05 x T_home`), so verifying is the dominant rational line, which is the P4 "costly but possible" shape. A pure phantom pickup (nothing there) is rejected as a glitch read: **a phantom never enters CARRIED** (Boarding F-6, OQ-BS-13). A class-(a) phantom has no subject, is never `lit` and takes no verb (IA-13); only a class-(d) lure, which is real junk, is carried, as itself, and its falsifiers while carried are the weight breath-step, SCAN and the trip home.
*Why.* The object is real, its mass is true, and the lie is falsifiable at a cost lower than the trip home (the concept's P4(d)).
*Edge notes.* Conflict C12 routed to Item DB: IDB:1341 OQ 27 calls the lure-band file "Perception-owned", IDB:333 "Perception and Sanity"; ruling: Sanity owns the data (it is a hallucination rule) and the Filter reads it. The six `# pending referenced_by` entries on Item DB and Suit constants (`weight_class`, `instance_mass_g`, `WEIGHT_MEDIUM_G`, `WEIGHT_HEAVY_G`, `WEIGHT_TELL_MIN_PM`, `STOWED_CUE_FLOOR_CLASS`) were converted to references by the registry stage (C8, closed 2026-10-27).

**SN-22. Class (a), phantom entities and hidden real entities.** *(Deferred: blocked on Horror Entities (In Design, draft on disk, horror-entities.md) and the Perception Filter.)*
*Rule.* **Phantoms** exist only in `sanity.perception`. A phantom has no SIM subject, is never registered with Emitter State (EM-21), and has no collision, noise, footprint, occlusion, trail or Attention input. It is drawn only inside the seat's own lit region as a decoy-class overlay within `DECOY_OVERLAYS_MAX` 4 (cited), with `PHANTOMS_MAX[3]` 2 `[PLACEHOLDER]` and the constraint `PHANTOMS_MAX + active decoy lies <= DECOY_OVERLAYS_MAX`. Onset is stage 3 only, `PHANTOM_GAP_S` 60 s apart, on a compartment-entry batch, life `PHANTOM_LIFE_S` 8 to 20 s; intent LURK, STALK or RUSH names *what*, never *how* (the Filter animates). A phantom walks walkable tiles only (a phantom through a wall is a free tell and breaks "geometry never lies", LY-34); it may overlap the seat but never pushes, never raises dust and never collides, which is the falsifier. Phantoms may reach into the cone, close in, and be heard through Sanity's own sound family (SN-25) (INTENSE, user). They may co-occur with real tells and breaches, where they are hardest to separate (the Attention must-consider), and the onset chance is multiplied by `PHANTOM_CO_OCCUR_MULT` 2 `[PLACEHOLDER]` while `phase` is TELL or BREACH, but they never use `web_strand_*`, `vfx_tell_*` or the band slide (AV-18) and never create, replace, delay or fake a tell or a calm (AT-17). The cost of responding is real: running is `NOISE_PER_TICK` 4 against walk 1, which pushes the player toward a breach, so one phantom encounter's expected Attention cost is bounded to a quarter of `ATT_THRESHOLD` (a design bound, so phantoms push toward a breach and never certainly cause one).
**Hidden real entity (HIDE).** A HIDE entry names a real HORROR ref. The entity stays registered (E6), keeps depositing M17 footprints (the web vocabulary, ART 5.5), keeps obeying AT-15 and keeps its own sounds; only its **body is undrawn for that seat** (CON:243 and Horror HE-32: "hides the body, never the trail"). `HIDE_MAX` 1 per seat, life `HIDE_LIFE_S` 8 to 20 s. A HIDE is ineligible or collapses at the next run when the entity is within `HIDE_SAFE_R` 288 px (6 tiles) of the seat, when Attention `phase` is BREACH or SUBSIDING with the entity in the locus, or when `horror.view` reports the entity's state outside {LURK, RETREAT} (STALK is Horror's additive sub-tell, so a hidden horror can never skip its tell). Horror's draft guarantees that outside BREACH or SUBSIDING-in-locus a horror never comes within `LURK_STANDOFF_PX` (HE-11, HE-12, HE-17; range 144..288, default 192 [PLACEHOLDER], registry `LURK_STANDOFF_PX`) of a living seat; `HIDE_SAFE_R` is strictly larger so a hidden thing is never adjacent and closing. **Build assertion:** `HIDE_SAFE_R > LURK_STANDOFF_PX`, evaluated at boot and on every retune of either knob (boot reason `SANITY_BOOT_HIDE_STANDOFF`); at Horror's top of range (288) the default `HIDE_SAFE_R` 288 would fail it, so Horror's retune forces a Sanity retune *(provisional — Horror Entities In Design, draft on disk)*.
*Falsifiers.* The footprint on the floor (a phantom leaves none), the absence of any Attention tell, walking through it (risk), a crewmate, time. **Shadow is not a real-versus-phantom test**: Horror's HE-2 states that a real horror contributes no occluder, casts no shadow, emits nothing and never changes `lux`, which resolves the art bible's 5.4 "occlude the beam, cast shadows" bullet against LO:151 rule 10 and EM-20 (conflict C4: resolved by HE-2 in the Horror draft, Horror's own rows say "C4 resolved by HE-2"; the art-bible bullet refresh is owed to the art-director) and preserves "a shadow with no occluder" as an unambiguous stage-2 sanity tell.
*Why.* The lie costs the player Attention and nerve, not HP; the entity stays a registered, footprint-leaving, tell-obeying thing, so Pillars 1 and 2 hold. Phantoms with SIM presence (they would violate H5 and EM-21) are rejected.
*Edge notes.* A phantom spawned while Attention is CALM leaves `attention.view`, `TellAudit` and `emitters()` unchanged (AC-AT-73; Sanity supplies the double). The lighting decoy kinds `GLOW_EXTENSION` and `PHANTOM_SHADOW` are VS-A content; which the Filter ships, and when, is Sanity's (LO OQ 16). The MVP reaches light only through the lamp-housing module-body channel (SN-16, stage 2).

#### Perception, determinism, audio, co-op and what is never touched

**SN-23. The perception content contract and the first frame.**
*Rule.* `sanity.perception` lists, per seat: `stage`, active lies `{subject_ref, kind, params, start_run, active_run, end_run, leak_modes, first_lie}` (SN-16 lie timing; `leak_modes` always contains GLEAM and the motion bit, SN-15 L-L) and, VS-A, phantom, hide and lure lists. It states **WHAT and WHEN**, never HOW (no pixels, shaders, alphas, easing). Every entry carries a non-empty leak (SN-15 L-L). It is rebuilt **in full** every run and in `post_restore` (CR-8: `persist false` means derived, rebuilt after load). Lint: no symbol of `sanity.perception` or `sanity.ledger` is read outside `sanity/` except, respectively, the Perception Filter / replication adapter and the Debug Overlay (DIAGNOSTIC). H5 (KRN:125) and CR-12 assert it. `post_restore` rebuilds from the restored `sanity.state` and `sanity.ledger` (`restore_after` places the ledger after the Graph and Layout blocks so subject refs resolve, SL-15 item 8); an unresolvable ref in the ledger is a hard `SANITY_RESTORE_*` failure and is never clamped.
*Why.* This answers SL H4, "no clean frame": one unfiltered frame would be a free oracle. Sanity claims **co-ownership of SL OQ-11 with the Perception Filter and the Pipeline** (C9) and states its half: the perception data is ready before the first RUNNING frame (AC-SL-93).
*Edge notes.* The soft cycle Sanity to Filter (IDX:267) resolves by one-way ownership: Sanity writes only perception state; the Filter writes only `CanvasItem` and material properties and never calls back. The Filter's per-stage onset smoothing is its own, subject to PR-13 (at most 3 flashes in a rolling 1 s) and "no uncaused pixel motion". **Ended entries are not retained** (Perception Filter OQ-PF-9): an ended lie leaves `sanity.perception` on the same run (SN-16), so loading clears the Filter's presentation-local glide-return tail; the first RUNNING frame after a restore shows the true value behind the black hold with no lie active to hide, so it is not a clean-frame oracle, and the residual is accepted.

**SN-24. Determinism, restore and no reroll oracle (answers SL OQ-37).**
*Rule.* Every draw is `rng_derive(world_seed, "sanity.<stream>/<PlayerId>", counter)` (for the lie stream the key is `"sanity.lie/<PlayerId>/<stable_id>"` with one counter per subject) or `rng_int(lo, hi)` (the kernel's D.10 functions; no other RNG exists) with the counter inside `sanity.ledger`, persisted and restored exactly (SL H2). Counters advance only on player-driven events: a gated lit-entry event on that subject for `sanity.lie`, a pickup or lit-entry for `sanity.lure`, a compartment-entry batch for `sanity.phantom` and `sanity.hide`. Targets come from the live lit set. Draw consumption is fixed per outcome (1 on a rejected onset, 4 on an accepted one, D.5), so a replay is bit-exact. **No content depends on `restore_reason`** (SL-24; REG:93-101: no consumer uses `DEATH_STUB` to alter hallucination content), and the level rolls back with the world (SL H3). Quit and death therefore offer no reroll: restore is exact for the same actions. **Accepted residual:** identical actions after a death replay the same lie at the same moment, priced by a death, as the scrubber that fails at minute four is in the physical world (Pillar 1). **No memorised script** (SL OQ-37's second constraint): the replay is identical only for identical actions (route, timing, beam and the lit set); any deviation re-gates every draw (gap, cap, stage, immunity, debounce), and a remembered lie is still a lie the ladder prices, so repeated deaths buy foreknowledge of one moment, never a fixed hallucination script.
*Why.* It satisfies SL H2, the registry rule and G3 / G4 (KRN:123, D.12) and is fully testable. Re-keying after restore is rejected: keyed from `restore_reason` it breaks SL-24; keyed from a PROFILE epoch counter it needs a PROFILE block while zero ship in the MVP (SL:285); and any re-key triggered by quit-and-Continue is a steerable reroll.
*Edge notes.* Per-subject visit counters (checkpoint CQ2, closes OQ-SN-14 for this GDD) mean no unrelated lit-entry elsewhere can shift a subject's dice: a deliberate pre-burn in the next room changes nothing, which removes the steering oracle, the clause of SL OQ-37 that matters. The residual is that "this subject lies on its third visit" is a per-seed fact, priced by a death (SL H3, the accepted residual above) and different in every world; the realised lie still varies with route and timing because the gates (gap, cap, stage, immunity, debounce) are dynamic. The counter-keying choice stays an ADR candidate. The engine reference has no scripting or core module file; GDScript integer overflow, signed-shift behaviour and Dictionary iteration order are **[UNV]**, so the ledger snapshot and `state_hash` must iterate sorted keys, and any `visit_key` or counter must stay below 2^53 (SL S-1).

**SN-25. The audio authorisation.**
*Rule.* Sanity authorises a **hallucination sound family** (the reading-lie sound signature of HR-5; phantom movement), owned and mixed by Adaptive Audio & Tells, disjoint from the tell family, the Graph loop sounds and Horror's sub-tell. Consequence-channel sounds, the human voice and the P1 proximity cue are never altered or faked. Adaptive Audio answers whether audio can lie (AA-1, AA-35: world sounds are ground truth, except the single `hal` family); any audio corruption is within the Filter's write set under the same rules.
*Why.* HR-5 requires a sound signature on every lying reading (ART:976). Adaptive Audio & Tells is designed (`adaptive-audio-and-tells.md`) and a hard edge in the systems index (IDX:202).

**SN-26. Co-op: per-seat independence (VS-B, designed, dormant).**
*Rule.* Per-seat levels are independent. The replicated summary is `{stage, tell_on, harm_eligible}` only; the ledger is HOST_ONLY; perception is OWNER_PRIVATE. No sanity appears on any crew strip or HUD surface (IDX:52). A saner crewmate aids recovery (SN-8) and is the third rung of the ladder. Voice, chat and beam are never altered. Everything is keyed by PlayerId, never `peer_id` (KRN:52, 100). Transport, validation and the replication adapter are Co-op's. A seat rebinding keeps its stored state (SN-3).
*Why.* The victim must see the attacker's tell (KRN:202) without any client learning another client's lies.

**SN-27. What Sanity never touches (a closed list, restated as a lint list).**
*Rule.* `body.state` and positions; walls, door leaves, seams and every geometry (LY-34, MSA F1); trails and Failure & Trail output; every consequence-channel item (SN-15 L-C); Attention outputs, thresholds and `TellAudit`; prompts, durations and the gate order; save and pause surfaces and autosave timing (SL H5, H6); camera and controller values (PCI:42, PIPE C1 and C9: no movement, aim or channel value reads sanity; identical input at stages 0 to 3 gives byte-identical `pos`, `facing` and `aim_angle`); the human channel; emitters (`emitter.state` has no phantom entry, AC-AT-73); Suit damage logic, the limiter and the body cues; and the pixels of any trail, which are bit-identical with the Filter on or off (FT-2, AC-88).
*Why.* This is the single place a reviewer checks that a new feature did not smuggle in a stat penalty or a consequence-channel lie. Each item is also an AC in Acceptance Criteria.

**SN-28. Data flags and QA controls.**
*Rule.* `CLASSES_ENABLED` is a data bitmask (a = 1, b = 2, c = 4, d = 8); the MVP value is {b}. Classes a, c and d are specified to the same depth, testable with stubs, and their ACs are tagged deferred with a named blocker (Horror, Filter, Boarding, Co-op). If class (b) is turned off at runtime, live lies expire normally and no new onsets occur, with no snapshot format change. Debug-only `force_stage(player, stage)` and `force_level(player, mSU)` live in the dev-only source root (`overlay_source_root`, `src/debug/`), registered only when the `dev` feature tag is present (`overlay_build_gate`) (registry: `overlay_build_gate`, `overlay_source_root`; a runtime debug flag is rejected because release ACs inspect the export file list), are reached from the dev console and displayed by the Debug Overlay (display only, DO-1, OQ-DO-11), are never a player surface, bypass the dwell and the cap, and are the only exception to SN-9; a forced recompute moves directly to the target stage with the dwell saturated, leaves the ledger untouched, and sets the seat's **`forced`** diagnostic flag (a per-seat field of `sanity.state`, outside the `SHARED_SUMMARY` projection, persisted so a restored forced save still reads FORCED, cleared only by NEW_GAME, and always false in a release export, where no `force_*` symbol exists); the Overlay's FORCED row shows it (OQ-DO-4). The Debug Overlay reads `sanity.state` and `sanity.ledger` as DIAGNOSTIC (true value beside perceived) and shows telemetry counters (`eligible_entries_per_min`, lies shown, acted on, debunked and expired unseen), so the reference lit-entry rate can be replaced by a measured one; none of this is ever an accessibility feature.
*Why.* The MVP must be testable in a 30-minute session, so QA needs to put a tester at stage 2 at will.

#### Calibration rules

**SN-29. The ambient floor: calm waiting never opens the gate.**
*Rule.* Ambient exposure E1 (`RING_EXPOSURE[ring]` plus `ABOARD_EXPOSURE`) contributes only while `level > AMBIENT_FLOOR[ring]`; below the floor E1 is 0 and recovery lifts the level back toward it. E2 and E3 are **not** floored. `RING_EXPOSURE[1..3]` = 0 / 4 / 8 and `ABOARD_EXPOSURE` 20 mSU per run (safe range 8 to 20), `AMBIENT_FLOOR[1..3]` = 62000 / 52000 / 43000 mSU, `AMBIENT_MARGIN` 5000 (all `[PLACEHOLDER]`; rings are Attention's AT-6 indexing with 1 the shallowest, the ring is `ring_of(actor_section(actor))`, a home body has `ABOARD_EXPOSURE` 0, and a home in ring 1 has E1 = 0). Three build assertions (formula `ambient_floor_invariant`): (a) `min(AMBIENT_FLOOR) >= SAN_TELL_OFF + AMBIENT_MARGIN`, so ambient exposure alone can never set `tell_on`, enter stage 3 or reach `harm_eligible`; (b) every floor lies at least `STAGE_HYST` from every stage entry and exit threshold, so a floor parked on a boundary never chatters the stage; (c) the floor is non-increasing with ring, so deeper is never safer (Pillar 5). A ring with no floor refuses boot with a closed `SANITY_BOOT_*` reason; a body whose ring is unknown (an unresolved section, or a ring below 1 that Web Map's block rejects at `post_restore`) uses ring 1's floor (the AT-6 fallback) and logs *(provisional — Web Map & Transit In Design, draft on disk)*.
*Why.* *(Orchestrator ruling: additive to R6; the values stay placeholders.)* Without floors, at `ABOARD_EXPOSURE` 20 and `REC_IDLE` 1, a player standing quietly aboard a derelict nets 19 mSU per run: stage 1 after 1579 runs (315.8 s), stage 3 after 75000 / 19 = 3948 runs (789.6 s, 13.2 min), and `SAN_HARM_ENTER` after 85000 / 19 = 4474 runs (894.8 s, 14.9 min) plus the dwell, with no danger at all. That contradicts the rulings' own sentence that stage 3 is reachable "only by sustained BREACH or horror contact" and the concept's "slow and costly". The floor makes the descent below stage 1 or 2 require events (Attention, horror), never waiting.
*Edge notes.* The calibration hypotheses (checkpoint CQ5) are `ABOARD_EXPOSURE` 20 giving stage 1 after 1579 runs = 315.8 s (about 5.3 min aboard at tier 0) and the ring-1 floor 62000 after 38000 / 19 = 2000 runs = 400 s (6.7 min); a settled home recovers 30000 mSU in 2500 runs (500 s); both are **playtest hypotheses, not facts**, and the safe range of `ABOARD_EXPOSURE` is 8 to 20 (playtest decides; this converts OQ-SN-1 into a knob range). A damaged home's worst ambient state is its ring's floor (CQ3), never lower without events. A seat returning home from a sortie that ended at the ring-1 floor (62000, stage 1) needs 10000 / 12 = 834 runs (167 s) to cross the stage-1 exit at 72000, so **lies on the home ship during that "hangover" are intended**: the player verifies in the place with every fault class's witnesses, and the consequence there is a home-ship repair, not a death. **Nesting with the sortie envelopes (Boarding C-BS-12):** the five-minute design target (300 s) <= stage-1 entry 315.8 s (15.8 s of slack) < Web Map's `T_SORTIE_SAFE_TICKS` 28,800 ticks (480 s) < `CELL_S`; a sortie at the fuel-floor bound spends about 164 s in stage 1. That is accepted: the fuel floor is Web Map's and looser than the sanity budget, the world shows the sanity cost and no timer does, and `ABOARD_EXPOSURE` is not retuned for it (OQ-SN-30 keeps it a playtest hypothesis).

**SN-30. Horror-contact budget: one horror cannot be farmed.**
*Rule.* E3 pays `EXP_HORROR` per run only against a per-horror **encounter budget** `HORROR_ENC_BUDGET` 3000 mSU `[PLACEHOLDER]`, tracked in `horror_enc[]` (ref, spent, last_seen_run; at most `HORROR_TRACK_MAX` 4 refs `[PLACEHOLDER]`). An encounter ends, and its budget resets, when that horror has been out of the seat's range (not within `HORROR_NEAR_R` 288 px on the same body) for `HORROR_ENC_RESET_S` 120 s. One horror therefore contributes at most 3000 mSU per encounter, so reaching `SAN_HARM_ENTER` from stage-1 entry by one horror alone takes at least 55000 / 3000 = 18.3 encounters, each separated by two minutes of not seeing it. Per-horror refs are SIM refs (EM-21).
*Why.* *(Orchestrator ruling: additive to R6.)* A repelled horror held at the edge of `HORROR_NEAR_R` would otherwise pay 20 mSU per run indefinitely, an unbounded farm for deliberate self-lowering (the concept's "watch for players lowering their own sanity deliberately"). "Sustained horror contact" in the rulings' calibration sentence therefore reads "repeated horror encounters".
*Edge notes.* Provisional on Horror Entities (In Design, draft on disk): it publishes `horror.view` (HE-37) positions and states and computes no exposure (HE-32, HE-40, AC-HE-101); Sanity owns `HORROR_NEAR_R`, `EXP_HORROR` and this budget. The seat's own noise and visibility remain Attention's.

### States and Transitions

Every transition below is evaluated inside the `sanity.drift` run (SN-2) in the fixed order given there, per seat, and is caused by the hook, by one of the two Class B mutators, or by the debug controls. **No player action, no other simulation system, and no presentation system can cause a transition directly**; the player affects the level only through the true world (SN-5, SN-8) and affects a lie only through a falsification or a real repair (SN-17). "Who may cause" is therefore stated as the single caller.

#### Stage machine (per seat; formula D.2)

| State | Entry condition (strictly below / strictly above; `stage_dwell >= STAGE_DWELL_RUNS` 25 or forced) | Exit condition | Transitions | Who may cause |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0 SANE | initial; or from 1 when `level > 72000` | `level < 70000` | to 1 | hook run (D.2); `force_*` (debug) |
| 1 INSTRUMENTS | from 0 at `level < 70000`; from 2 when `level > 47000` | `level < 45000`, or `level > 72000` | to 2; to 0 | hook run; `force_*` |
| 2 LIGHT | from 1 at `level < 45000`; from 3 when `level > 27000` | `level < 25000`, or `level > 47000` | to 3; to 1 | hook run; `force_*` |
| 3 WORLD | from 2 at `level < 25000` | `level > 27000` | to 2 | hook run; `force_*` |

Rules: worsening is evaluated before recovery; the stage moves directly to the target when the dwell has expired (it never steps one at a time unless the level sits in the adjacent band); one transition per run, the deeper applying if two dwell timers would expire together; `stage_dwell` resets to 0 on every change and counts up by one per run, saturating at `STAGE_DWELL_RUNS`; a level exactly on a threshold holds the current stage; a restore re-uses the stored `stage` and `stage_dwell` (no recompute); `force_*` recomputes with the dwell saturated. Reach per stage: SN-16.

#### Victim-tell flag `tell_on` (per seat)

| State | Entry | Exit | Who may cause |
|---|---|---|---|
| `tell_on` = false | initial; `level > 35000` (`SAN_TELL_OFF`) | `level < 30000` (`SAN_TELL_ON`) | hook run |
| `tell_on` = true | `level < 30000` | `level > 35000` | hook run |

No dwell: the flag is a lead indicator, set early. It is read through `sanity_view` by the Filter (the victim-side fringe, class c), Adaptive Audio & Tells (presentation only) and the Debug Overlay (DIAGNOSTIC); no simulation system reads it.

#### Harm gate `harm_eligible` (per seat; formula D.3)

| State | Entry | Exit | Counter `harm_below_runs` | Who may cause |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| NOT_ELIGIBLE, counting | a run with `level < 15000` (`SAN_HARM_ENTER`) | any run with `level >= 15000` (counter resets to 0) | +1 per run, up to 150 | hook run |
| ELIGIBLE | `harm_below_runs >= 150` (`SAN_HARM_DWELL_RUNS`, 30 s) | any run with `level > 20000` (`SAN_HARM_EXIT`): clears at once, counter 0 | frozen at 150 while eligible | hook run; `force_*` (debug) |

A restore landing mid-dwell resumes the persisted counter; the bit does not reset.

#### Lie lifecycle (per ledger entry; SN-16, SN-17)

| State | Entered when | Leaves to | In `sanity.perception`? | Who may cause |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| (none) | none | PENDING on an accepted onset draw | no | hook run (D.5) |
| PENDING | D.5 accepts; delay of `ONSET_DELAY_RUNS` 10 to 25 runs set | ACTIVE after the delay if the SN-15 re-check passes; INVALIDATED if not | no | hook run |
| ACTIVE | delay elapsed and re-check ok | EXPIRED at `end_run`; DEBUNKED; INVALIDATED | yes, with `leak_modes` | hook run |
| EXPIRED | `run >= end_run` | removed; no immunity | no | hook run (the time rung) |
| DEBUNKED | `record_falsification` on the subject, or a full-channel repair or inspect | removed; immunity for `DEBUNK_IMMUNE_S` | no | `record_falsification` (Interaction's apply) |
| INVALIDATED | any SN-15 re-check fails (witness ended, atmosphere band left OK, a fixed gauge relied on at activation destroyed, true severity worse than `onset_stage` allows), the subject is destroyed or salvaged, the true state now equals the displayed state, or the class is disabled by data | removed; no immunity (a destroyed subject cannot be immune) | no | hook run |

A stage change does not end a lie (SN-16). Expiry is evaluated before a check on the same tick (SN-17 edge notes).

#### Compartment-entry state `entry_pending` (per seat; SN-16)

| State | Entered when | Left when | Who may cause |
|---|---|---|---|
| clear | initial; the batch fired; the seat left the compartment | the seat's compartment changes | hook run |
| set | the seat's compartment changed | the first run at which the seat's lit candidate set is non-empty (the batch fires and every candidate enters together), or the seat leaves that compartment | hook run |

#### Seat lifecycle (SN-3)

| State | Drift | Onset | Ledger | Entered by |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| UNBOUND | frozen | none | retained | the seat is absent (VS-B) |
| BOUND_ALIVE | D.1 | yes | live | bind |
| DOWNED | D.1 | yes | live | Suit DOWNED |
| DEAD | frozen | none | frozen | Suit DEAD; in the MVP the death stub reloads the last autosave (SL-7), replacing the frozen state with the restored one |

The seat state is read from Suit (DOWNED, DEAD) and the session's binding table; Sanity never sets it.

### Interactions with Other Systems

`(provisional — <dep> undesigned/in flight)` marks a neighbour with no GDD, or one whose GDD is in flight; this GDD is the first writer of its half, and lists only what Sanity supplies or needs (reflexion P-4). Where the neighbour is designed, its own Interactions row is **quoted** so the two documents can be checked against each other. Sibling GDDs are uncommitted in this run, so line references are re-grepped at the registry stage.
