## Formulas

*Conventions (read first; every formula below uses them).* **Units.** The level is in **mSU** (integer); one **run** is one `sanity.drift` invocation (`SANITY_DRIFT_DIVISOR` 12 ticks = 0.2 s, 5 runs per second); rates are mSU per run; durations in seconds are `runs / 5`. `clamp(x, lo, hi) = max(lo, min(hi, x))`; `div` is floor division and is applied to non-negative operands only; `ceil(a / b)` for `a >= 0` is `(a + b - 1) div b`. **Integer rule (kernel determinism).** Everything that is hashed, persisted or replicated is integer: D.1 to D.6 and D.8 to D.10 are integer and run inside the hook or at boot. D.7 and D.11 to D.13 are **design-time and QA formulas**: they are seconds-valued or fractional because nothing in them is hashed, persisted or replicated, they never run in the sim, and a float appears only in them and in the test oracles. **Placeholders used in the worked examples** (all `[PLACEHOLDER]`, tuned in `assets/data/sanity/`): `SAN_MAX` 100000, `STAGE_ENTER[1..3]` 70000 / 45000 / 25000, `STAGE_HYST` 2000, `STAGE_DWELL_RUNS` 25, `SAN_TELL_ON` 30000, `SAN_TELL_OFF` 35000, `SAN_HARM_ENTER` 15000, `SAN_HARM_EXIT` 20000, `SAN_HARM_DWELL_RUNS` 150, `DRIFT_MAX_PER_RUN` 36, `REC_SETTLED` 12, `REC_IDLE` 1, `REC_CREW` 3, `REC_MAX` 15, `PENDING_MAX` 3000, `RING_EXPOSURE[1..3]` 0 / 4 / 8, `ABOARD_EXPOSURE` 20, `AMBIENT_FLOOR[1..3]` 62000 / 52000 / 43000, `EXP_TELL[1..3]` 6 / 12 / 20, `EXP_BREACH` 28, `EXP_SUBSIDING` 14, `EXP_BREACH_ENTRY` 1500, `EXP_HORROR` 20. **Cited, never re-derived:** KRN D.10 (`rng_derive`, `unit`, `rng_int`), the Graph's `HP_OK` 7000 and `HP_FAIL` 3000, `FIELD_CHECK_TICKS` 120, `HARM_CHANNEL_TICKS` 120, `HARM_TOTAL_BP` 10000, `V_WALK` 2 px per tick, `TILE_PX` 48, `REACH_PX` 72, `NOISE_PER_TICK` (walk 1 / run 4 / carry 1) and `ATT_THRESHOLD` 6000 AU, `WEIGHT_TELL_MIN_PM` 300, `WEIGHT_MEDIUM_G` 5000, `WEIGHT_HEAVY_G` 25000. **Evaluation order inside one run** is SN-2 steps (1) to (7): inputs, D.1, D.2 and D.3, ledger upkeep, lit-entry events and D.5, perception rebuild, `lit_prev`. Hash outputs in the D.5 and D.6 examples are illustrative until the kernel's `mix64` ADR picks the function (as KRN D.10).

#### D.1 `sanity_drift_step`

The `sanity_drift_step` formula is defined as:

`E1 = (level > AMBIENT_FLOOR[ring]) ? RING_EXPOSURE[ring] + (aboard ? ABOARD_EXPOSURE : 0) : 0` (in the pod, `ring` is Attention's `held_ring` and `aboard` is false, SN-5 E1); `E2 = {CALM: 0, TELL: EXP_TELL[tier], BREACH: EXP_BREACH, SUBSIDING: EXP_SUBSIDING}[phase]`; `E3 = sum over near horrors h of min(EXP_HORROR, HORROR_ENC_BUDGET - spent_h)`; `E = E1 + E2 + E3 + E4`; `R = (settled ? REC_SETTLED : REC_IDLE) + (crew_saner ? REC_CREW : 0)`; `base = E - R`; `draw = min(pending, max(0, DRIFT_MAX_PER_RUN - base))`; `net = clamp(base + draw, -REC_MAX, DRIFT_MAX_PER_RUN)`; `level' = clamp(level - net, 0, SAN_MAX)`; `pending' = min(PENDING_MAX, pending - draw + startle_add)`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `level` | int mSU | 0 to `SAN_MAX` | `sanity.state` | the seat's level at the start of the run; the only state this formula lowers |
| `ring` | int | 1 to 3 | `ring(PlayerId)` = `ring_of(actor_section(actor))`, AT-6, WM-27 *(provisional — Web Map & Transit In Design, draft on disk)*; Attention's `held_ring` while the seat is IN_POD | depth ring of the seat's section; unknown resolves to 1 |
| `aboard` | bool | - | `not body_is_home` and `loco_state` is not IN_POD (WM-27, OQ-WM-6) *(provisional — Web Map & Transit In Design, draft on disk)* | the seat's body is a foreign body; false in the pod, so a pod hop is never charged `ABOARD_EXPOSURE` |
| `RING_EXPOSURE[ring]` | int mSU/run | 0 / 4 / 8 | knob | depth term of E1 |
| `ABOARD_EXPOSURE` | int mSU/run | 20 (safe 8 to 20) | knob | foreign-body term of E1 |
| `AMBIENT_FLOOR[ring]` | int mSU | 62000 / 52000 / 43000 | knob (SN-29) | E1 contributes only while `level` is strictly above it; E2 and E3 are never floored |
| `phase`, `tier` | enum, int | CALM/TELL/BREACH/SUBSIDING; 0 to 3 | `attention.view` (AT-16), read one-way | `tier` 0 in TELL contributes 0 |
| `EXP_TELL[tier]` | int mSU/run | 6 / 12 / 20 for tier 1 to 3 | knob | tell-phase rate |
| `EXP_BREACH`, `EXP_SUBSIDING` | int mSU/run | 28, 14 | knob | breach and subsiding rates |
| `near horror h`, `spent_h` | set, int mSU | up to `HORROR_TRACK_MAX` 4 refs; 0 to `HORROR_ENC_BUDGET` 3000 | `horror.view` positions (HE-37), `horror_enc[]` *(provisional — Horror Entities In Design, draft on disk)* | horror within `HORROR_NEAR_R` on the same body, and its encounter spend (D.10) |
| `E4` | int | 0 | Co-op (dormant) | witnessed crew death; 0 until VS-B |
| `settled` | bool | - | Body (`body_is_home`), Graph bands of the seat's compartment, Attention `phase`/`tier` | home body AND all three atmosphere bands OK AND `phase` CALM AND `tier` 0 (SN-8) |
| `crew_saner` | bool | - | Co-op (dormant) | another bound seat within `CREW_R` is at least one stage saner |
| `REC_SETTLED`, `REC_IDLE`, `REC_CREW` | int mSU/run | 12, 1, 3 | knobs | recovery terms |
| `REC_MAX` | int mSU/run | 15 | derived: `REC_SETTLED + REC_CREW` | lower clamp of `net`; unreachable by construction, kept as a build assertion |
| `DRIFT_MAX_PER_RUN` | int mSU/run | 36 (<= 36 per D.4) | knob | the net cap |
| `pending` | int mSU | 0 to `PENDING_MAX` 3000 | `sanity.state` | undrained startle debt |
| `startle_add` | int mSU | 0 or `EXP_BREACH_ENTRY` 1500 | the run where `tell_epoch` differs from `last_tell_epoch` and `tell_outcome` is BREACHED | the only way `pending` grows |
| `net` | int mSU/run | -15 to +36 | result | positive means loss |

**Output Range:** `net` is -15 (settled home, saner crewmate, `E` = 0) to +36 (any `base + draw` at or above 36). `level'` is in `[0, SAN_MAX]` and `pending'` in `[0, PENDING_MAX]`. `E` can reach 76 with one horror near (`8 + 20 + 28 + 20`), so the cap, not the sum, is the binding bound, and the lower clamp never binds (`E >= 0`, `R <= 15`). Nothing outside D.1 writes `level` except the debug `force_level` (SN-9). The drain is `draw`, which can exceed the visible level change when `base` is negative (the settled-home startle repays faster than it hurts); a zero `pending` makes `draw` 0.
**Example:** (all at `phase`/`tier` shown, `pending` 0 unless stated)
- A. Ring 1 derelict, TELL tier 2, `level` 80000: `E1 = 0 + 20 = 20`, `E2 = 12`, `E = 32`, `R = 1` (aboard, not settled), `base = 31`, `draw = 0`, `net = 31`; `level'` = 79969.
- B. The same at `level` 60000, below the ring-1 floor 62000: `E1 = 0`, `E = 12`, `base = 11`, `net = 11`; `level'` = 59989. The floor removes only the ambient term.
- C. Ring 3 derelict, BREACH, one horror near, `pending` 1500, `level` 80000: `E1 = 8 + 20 = 28`, `E2 = 28`, `E3 = 20`, `E = 76`, `R = 1`, `base = 75`, `draw = min(1500, max(0, 36 - 75)) = 0`, `net = clamp(75, -15, 36) = 36`; `level'` = 79964, `pending'` 1500 (the cap binds and the startle waits).
- D. Home body, ring 1, settled, `level` 50000: `E = 0`, `R = 12`, `base = -12`, `net = -12`; `level'` = 50012.
- E. Home body, ring 1, the run BREACH is entered (`tell_outcome` BREACHED, new epoch), `level` 90000, `pending` 0: `E1 = 0`, `E2 = 28`, `E = 28`, `R = 1` (BREACH is not CALM), `base = 27`, `draw = 0`, `net = 27`; `level'` = 89973, `pending'` = 1500. Next run, BREACH persisting: `draw = min(1500, 36 - 27) = 9`, `net = 36`, `pending'` = 1491; draining all 1500 at headroom 9 takes `ceil(1500 / 9)` = 167 runs = 33.4 s if BREACH lasts that long (orchestrator correction of the rulings' "about 42 runs", which assumes zero other exposure).
- F. Ring 1 derelict, BREACH, `pending` 1500, `level` 80000: `E = 20 + 28 = 48`, `base = 47`, `draw = 0`, `net = 36`; the startle waits until BREACH ends.
- G. Home body ring 1, SUBSIDING, `pending` 1500: `E = 14`, `R = 1`, `base = 13`, `draw = min(1500, 23) = 23`, `net = 36`; the debt clears in `ceil(1500 / 23)` = 66 runs = 13.2 s.
- H. Home body, settled, saner crewmate within `CREW_R` (VS-B): `E = 0`, `R = 15`, `net = -15`; `level'` = 50015.
- I. Seat IN_POD with `held_ring` 1, phase CALM, `level` 80000: `aboard` false, `E1 = 0`, `E = 0`, `settled` false so `R = REC_IDLE` = 1, `base = -1`, `net = -1`; `level'` = 80001. The same seat docked aboard derelict A: `E1 = 20`, `base = 19`, `net = 19`.

#### D.2 `stage_of`

The `stage_of` formula is defined as:

`stage' = stage_of(level, stage, stage_dwell)`: return `stage` while `stage_dwell < STAGE_DWELL_RUNS`; otherwise step `target` deeper while `level < STAGE_ENTER[target + 1]` (worsening is evaluated first), and only if it did not move, step `target` shallower while `level > STAGE_ENTER[target] + STAGE_HYST`

The same rule as a step list (one transition per run, the deeper applying):

```
if stage_dwell < STAGE_DWELL_RUNS: return stage
target = stage
while target < 3 and level < STAGE_ENTER[target + 1]: target += 1      # worsen first
if target == stage:
    while target > 0 and level > STAGE_ENTER[target] + STAGE_HYST: target -= 1
return target                                                          # caller resets stage_dwell to 0 on a change
```

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `level` | int mSU | 0 to 100000 | `sanity.state` | level after D.1 |
| `stage` | int | 0 to 3 | `sanity.state` | stage before this run |
| `stage_dwell` | int runs | 0 to `STAGE_DWELL_RUNS` (saturating) | `sanity.state` | runs since the last change; +1 per run |
| `STAGE_ENTER[s]` | int mSU | 70000 / 45000 / 25000 for s = 1..3 | knob | entry thresholds, strictly below |
| `STAGE_HYST` | int mSU | 2000 (safe 1000 to 4000) | knob | the exit threshold of stage `s` is `STAGE_ENTER[s] + STAGE_HYST` (72000 / 47000 / 27000), strictly above |
| `STAGE_DWELL_RUNS` | int runs | 25 (5 s; safe 10 to 50) | knob | minimum runs between changes |

**Output Range:** an integer in 0 to 3; a stage 4 is impossible by construction. Entry is strictly below and exit strictly above, so `level` exactly 70000 stays stage 0 and exactly 72000 stays stage 1. Two thresholds cannot be crossed inside one dwell under the cap (`25 x 36` = 900 mSU against gaps of at least 20000); the only ways to see a jump of more than one stage are a restore (the stored stage is kept, no recompute) and a debug `force_*` (recompute with the dwell saturated, directly to the target).
**Example:** `level` 69964, `stage` 0, `stage_dwell` 25: 69964 < 70000 so `target` 1; 69964 is not < 45000, so stage 1 and the dwell resets. At 71000 the stage holds (71000 is not above 72000). At 72001 with the dwell back at 25, `target` 0. Direct drop: `stage` 2, `level` 47500, dwell 25: not worsening; 47500 > 47000 so `target` 1; 47500 is not > 72000 so it stops at 1.

#### D.3 `harm_gate`

The `harm_gate` formula is defined as:

`harm_below_runs' = (level < SAN_HARM_ENTER) ? min(SAN_HARM_DWELL_RUNS, harm_below_runs + 1) : 0` while not eligible; `harm_eligible' = harm_eligible ? (level <= SAN_HARM_EXIT) : (harm_below_runs' >= SAN_HARM_DWELL_RUNS)`; `tell_on' = (level < SAN_TELL_ON) ? true : (level > SAN_TELL_OFF) ? false : tell_on`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `SAN_HARM_ENTER` | int mSU | 15000 | knob | harm-gate entry threshold, strictly below |
| `SAN_HARM_EXIT` | int mSU | 20000 (> `SAN_HARM_ENTER`; safe 15360, the D.9 boot assertion, to 25000) | knob | exit threshold, strictly above, clears at once |
| `SAN_HARM_DWELL_RUNS` | int runs | 150 (30 s; safe 50 to 300) | knob | consecutive runs below `SAN_HARM_ENTER` needed |
| `harm_below_runs` | int runs | 0 to 150 | `sanity.state` | dwell counter; frozen at 150 while eligible and reset to 0 on clearing |
| `SAN_TELL_ON`, `SAN_TELL_OFF` | int mSU | 30000, 35000 (`SAN_TELL_OFF` > `SAN_TELL_ON`) | knobs | victim-tell flag thresholds; no dwell |
| `harm_eligible`, `tell_on` | bool | - | `sanity.state` | the two flags; `harm_eligible` is the single SIM bit the gate query and the class-(c) render read (SN-11) |

**Output Range:** two booleans and a counter in 0 to 150. The earliest eligibility is 150 runs = 30 s after the first run below 15000. Outside debug force: `harm_eligible` implies `stage == 3` (eligibility needs the level below 20000 and stage 3 is held until 27000) and implies `tell_on` (cleared only above 35000), asserted at boot as corollaries of D.4.
**Example:** the level crosses 14990 at run n: counter 1; at run n + 149 the counter reaches 150 and the bit sets (30 s). If instead the level returns to exactly 15000 at run n + 60 (counter 60), the counter resets to 0 (a run at or above `SAN_HARM_ENTER` resets). Once eligible at 9600, recovery to 20001 clears the bit that run; at 20000 exactly it holds. `tell_on` sets at 29999 and, at 33000, stays set (inside the band).

#### D.4 `drift_lead_invariant`

The `drift_lead_invariant` formula is defined as:

`lead_runs(a, b) = (a - b + DRIFT_MAX_PER_RUN - 1) div DRIFT_MAX_PER_RUN`; `lead_s(a, b) = lead_runs(a, b) x SANITY_DRIFT_DIVISOR / TICK_RATE`; build assertions (i) `lead_s(STAGE_ENTER[1], SAN_TELL_ON) >= SAN_STAGE1_TO_TELL_MIN_S`, (ii) `lead_s(SAN_TELL_ON, SAN_HARM_ENTER) >= SAN_TELL_LEAD_S`, (iii) `lead_s(STAGE_ENTER[1], SAN_HARM_ENTER) >= SAN_MIN_ONSET_TO_HARM_S`, (iv) `lead_s(SAN_TELL_ON, SAN_HARM_ENTER) + SAN_HARM_DWELL_RUNS x SANITY_DRIFT_DIVISOR / TICK_RATE >= SAN_TELL_LEAD_S`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `a`, `b` | int mSU | 0 to 100000 | knobs (thresholds) | upper and lower level of a bound |
| `DRIFT_MAX_PER_RUN` | int mSU/run | 36 | knob | the net cap, the worst-case descent rate |
| `SANITY_DRIFT_DIVISOR` | int ticks | 12 | equals the kernel divisor by relation (KRN:54, KRN:202); not a knob | ticks per run |
| `SAN_STAGE1_TO_TELL_MIN_S` | int s | 220 | knob | required stage-1 to `tell_on` lead |
| `SAN_TELL_LEAD_S` | int s | 60 (safe 45 to 90) | knob | required `tell_on` to eligibility lead |
| `SAN_MIN_ONSET_TO_HARM_S` | int s | 300 (safe 240 to 420) | knob | required stage-1 entry to eligibility lead |
| `STAGE_ENTER[1]`, `SAN_TELL_ON`, `SAN_HARM_ENTER` | int mSU | 70000, 30000, 15000 | knobs | the `a` and `b` of bounds (i) to (iv) |
| `SAN_HARM_DWELL_RUNS` | int runs | 150 | knob | the dwell added in bound (iv) |

**Output Range:** pass or fail per bound. At the defaults:

| Bound | Distance (mSU) | Runs at cap 36 | Seconds | Required | Margin |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| (i) 70000 to 30000 | 40000 | 1112 | 222.4 | 220 | 2.4 s |
| (ii) 30000 to 15000 | 15000 | 417 | 83.4 | 60 | 23.4 s |
| (iii) 70000 to 15000 | 55000 | 1528 | 305.6 (the bit sets on run 1677 = 335.4 s with the dwell, SN-10) | 300 | 5.6 s |
| (iv) `SAN_TELL_ON` to eligible, with the 150-run dwell | 15000 | 417 + 150 = 567 | 113.4 | 60 | 53.4 s |

**Closed form for the cap.** Bound `i` needs `lead_runs >= 5 x S_i`, which holds exactly when `DRIFT_MAX_PER_RUN < d_i / (5 x S_i - 1)`: (i) `40000 / 1099` = 36.4, (ii) `15000 / 299` = 50.2, (iii) `55000 / 1499` = 36.7, so the largest passing cap is `min(36, 50, 36)` = 36 and 36 is the value used. At 37: (i) `ceil(40000 / 37)` = 1082 runs = 216.4 s fails, and (iii) `ceil(55000 / 37)` = 1487 runs = 297.4 s fails. (i) and (iii) bind together. **Cap-is-binding demonstration:** the uncapped `E - R` exceeds 36 in every danger state that matters: ring 3 aboard in BREACH with a horror near is `8 + 20 + 28 + 20 - 1` = 75; without the horror `8 + 20 + 28 - 1` = 55; ring 1 aboard in BREACH `0 + 20 + 28 - 1` = 47; ring 1 aboard in a tier-3 TELL `0 + 20 + 20 - 1` = 39; a tier-2 TELL aboard (`0 + 20 + 12 - 1` = 31) is the first state below the cap. **Example:** raise the cap to 40 with nothing else changed: (iii) gives `ceil(55000 / 40)` = 1375 runs = 275.0 s against 300 s, failing; the boot assertion refuses with `SANITY_BOOT_LEAD_INVARIANT`. Any retune of a threshold, the cap, the divisor, or an exposure rate re-runs all four (reflexion P-2, P-7).

#### D.5 `lie_onset`

The `lie_onset` formula is defined as:

`w = (u in repaired_recent within REPAIR_BIAS_S) ? REPAIR_BIAS_W : 1`; `p_pm = min(1000, ONSET_PM[s] x w)`; `x = rng_int(1, 1000)` at `c`; accept iff `x <= p_pm`; on accept `kind = weighted pick over the legal kinds by KIND_W[kind][s]` via `rng_int(1, sum_W)` at `c + 1`, `life_runs = rng_int(5 x 40, 5 x 120)` at `c + 2`, `delay_runs = rng_int(10, 25)` at `c + 3`, `visit[p][u] = c + 4`, `last_onset_run = run`; on reject `visit[p][u] = c + 1`

It is applied to each gated lit-entry event on subject `u` for seat `p` at stage `s`, in the SN-16 order, with `c = visit[p][u]` on the stream `"sanity.lie/<p>/<u>"`.

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `s` | int | 1 to 3 | `sanity.state` stage | stage 0 never reaches this formula (pre-draw gate) |
| `ONSET_PM[s]` | int permille | 150 / 300 / 550 (safe 50 to 800) | knob | accept chance of one gated event |
| `REPAIR_BIAS_S`, `REPAIR_BIAS_W` | int s, int | 60, 2 (safe 0 to 120; 1 to 3) | knobs | a subject repaired within the window is `W` times as likely to come undone |
| `visit[p][u]` | int | 0 to 2^53 | `sanity.ledger` map, pruned on subject destruction | counts only gated events on `u` for `p` (a failed gate draws nothing and advances nothing) |
| `KIND_W[kind][s]` | int | stage 1 = 45 / 25 / 30 / 0; stage 2 = 40 / 25 / 30 / 5; stage 3 = 25 / 30 / 15 / 30 for FALSE_OK / FALSE_FAIL / GLIDE / SPRITE_SWAP | knobs | weights over the kinds the subject's true state and reach allow (SN-16) |
| `LIE_LIFE_S` | int s | 40 to 120 (safe 20 to 180) | knob | the two ends of the life band; times 5 for runs |
| `ONSET_DELAY_RUNS` | int runs | 10 to 25 (safe 5 to 50) | knob | invisible PENDING delay before re-validation |
| `rng_int(lo, hi)` | int | inclusive, no modulo bias | KRN D.10 | on the stream key above, counter as shown |

**Output Range:** `p_pm` in 150 to 1000 (1000 only at stage 3 with weight 2: `min(1000, 1100)`); `life_runs` 200 to 600 (40 to 120 s); `delay_runs` 10 to 25 (2 to 5 s). Draw consumption is exactly 1 (reject) or 4 (accept) per gated event, so a replay is bit-exact (SL H2, G4), and `visit[p][u]` is a function of that seat's own actions on that subject only.
**Example:** stage 2 (`ONSET_PM` 300), a degraded gauge not recently repaired, third gated event on it (`visit` = 2), illustrative `x = 217` at counter 2: 217 <= 300 accept. Legal kinds for a degraded gauge at stage 2 are FALSE_OK 40 and GLIDE 30 (FALSE_FAIL needs a healthy subject, SPRITE_SWAP needs a glow sprite), `sum_W` 70; the roll at counter 3 is 25, so FALSE_OK. Counter 4: `rng_int(200, 600)` = 372 runs = 74.4 s. Counter 5: `rng_int(10, 25)` = 17 runs = 3.4 s. `visit` becomes 6. The same subject repaired 30 s earlier: `p_pm = min(1000, 300 x 2)` = 600, and x = 217 still accepts; x = 450 would now accept where it rejected before. Stage 3 with weight 2 and any x in 1 to 1000 accepts.

#### D.6 `lure_dressing` *(class d; VS-A; MVP ships none)*

The `lure_dressing` formula is defined as:

`candidates(j) = sorted by band_id { b in lure_bands : class(b.mid_g) != weight_class(m_j) AND |m_j - b.mid_g| x 1000 >= WEIGHT_TELL_MIN_PM x b.mid_g }`; `band(j) = candidates(j)[rng_int(0, |candidates(j)| - 1)]` on the stream `"sanity.lure/<PlayerId>"` at `c_lure`, then `c_lure' = c_lure + 1`; the result is stored in `lures[]` subject to `|lures[]| <= LURES_MAX[s]` and `|lures[]| <= (LURE_FRACTION_MAX_PM x n_junk_lit) div 1000`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `m_j` | int g | > 0 | Item DB `instance_mass_g` | true mass of the junk instance |
| `b.mid_g` | int g | > 0 | `assets/data/sanity/lure_bands.json` (Sanity-owned) | the mass the lure pretends to have |
| `weight_class` | enum | LIGHT / MEDIUM / HEAVY | Item DB; `WEIGHT_MEDIUM_G` 5000, `WEIGHT_HEAVY_G` 25000 | class of a mass |
| `WEIGHT_TELL_MIN_PM` | int permille of the lure mass | 300 | cited | smallest gap the breath-step can show |
| `LURES_MAX[s]` | int | 0 / 2 / 3 | knob | per seat, by stage |
| `LURE_FRACTION_MAX_PM` | int permille | 400 | knob | cap on the share of lit junk that may be dressed |
| `n_junk_lit` | int | 0+ | Emitter `lit` over junk in the seat's compartment | junk in the seat's lit set |
| `c_lure` | int | 0 to 2^53 | `sanity.ledger` | advances by 1 per dressing draw, on pickup or lit-entry only |

**Output Range:** a band id, or none when `candidates` is empty (the junk stays undressed: a lie with no weight witness is never created). Real valuables are never dressed as junk (anti-P5); a seat whose `cue_load` is HEAVY through stowed items gets no lure (SE OQ-8).
**Example:** junk 2000 g (LIGHT). Band "plate" `mid_g` 30000 (HEAVY): classes differ and `|2000 - 30000| x 1000` = 28,000,000 >= `300 x 30000` = 9,000,000, a candidate. Band "cell" 2600 (LIGHT): same class, excluded. Band "crate" 6000 (MEDIUM): `4,000 x 1000` = 4,000,000 >= `300 x 6000` = 1,800,000, a candidate. Junk 4800 g against band 5200 (MEDIUM): classes differ but `400 x 1000` = 400,000 < `300 x 5200` = 1,560,000, excluded (the breath-step could not show it). With two candidates the draw `rng_int(0, 1)` picks one; with 5 lit junk items, at most `(400 x 5) div 1000` = 2 are dressed.

#### D.7 `verification_cost_ladder` *(design-time; seconds-valued; every scan cost is a lower bound)*

The `verification_cost_ladder` formula is defined as:

`T_room = ceil(d_room / V_WALK) + T_look`; `T_second_fixed = ceil(d_second / V_WALK) + T_look`; `T_second_suit = 0`; `T_scan = ceil(d_reach / V_WALK) + T_EQUIP + FIELD_CHECK_TICKS`; `T_time = (end_run - run) x SANITY_DRIFT_DIVISOR`; seconds = `ticks / TICK_RATE`; break-even `d* = (FIELD_CHECK_TICKS / TICK_RATE) x (V_WALK x TICK_RATE / TILE_PX)` tiles

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `d_room` | int px | 0+ | Layout (the examples below are illustrative placements, not the table of record) | walk to the witness anchor |
| `d_second` | int px | 0+ | Layout | walk to another fixed gauge on the loop |
| `d_reach` | int px | 0 to a few `REACH_PX` | Layout, `REACH_PX` 72 (cited) | walk still needed to bring the lying surface within reach before a scan |
| `V_WALK` | int px/tick | 2 (cited) | pixel pipeline | walking speed: 120 px/s = 2.5 tiles/s at `TILE_PX` 48 |
| `T_look` | int ticks | Interaction's inspect or listen channel | Interaction | read-out time; 0 for a listened witness; carried as 0 in the lower bounds |
| `T_EQUIP` | int ticks | **0 until Suit registers a duration (OQ-SN-6)** | Suit SE-11 / Controller | swap to scanner slot 3; every scan cost below is therefore a **lower bound** |
| `FIELD_CHECK_TICKS` | int ticks | 120 (cited; safe 60 to 240) | Interaction | the check channel |
| `end_run`, `run` | int | - | `sanity.ledger` | the lie's expiry run and the current run; the time rung |

**Output Range:** non-negative ticks; every lie has at least two finite non-time rungs (L-W guarantees rung 1, L-2I rung 2) and the time rung is at most `5 x 120` runs x 12 = 7200 ticks = 120 s. **Break-even:** `d*` = 2 s x (2 x 60 / 48) = 2 s x 2.5 tiles/s = **5 tiles** one way (witness nearer than 5 tiles: look; farther: scan); at the 90-tick floor `FIELD_CHECK_FLOOR_TICKS` it is 3.75 tiles, at the 60-tick lower bound of `FIELD_CHECK_TICKS` 2.5 tiles, at 240 ticks 10 tiles. **Scan from where one stands, lower bound:** `T_scan = ceil(72 / 2) + 0 + 120` = 156 ticks = **2.6 s**.

**The eight MVP faults** (rung 1 distances illustrative; seconds = ticks / 60; a fixed gauge elsewhere costs `ceil(960 / 2)` = 480 ticks = 8.0 s of walk plus `T_look`):

| # | Fault | The lie (module-body only) | Rung 1, the room: witness, example d_room | Lit? | Noise | Rung 2, cheapest honest second instrument (lower bound) | Rung 4, time | Rung 3 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | O2 pinhole at a clamp | duct or scrubber gauge, state glyph | HISS plus AIRFLOW at the duct, 288 px = 144 ticks = 2.4 s | hear no, see yes | walk 1/tick | `suit.o2` at 0 s (current compartment); scanner 2.6 s; another O2 gauge 8.0 s + look | <= 120 s | trust, VS-B |
| 2 | O2 seam leak after a weld | pressure gauge, hatch glyph | SEAL_STRAIN creak and rim frost, 192 px = 96 ticks = 1.6 s | no | walk | `suit.o2` 0 s; scanner 2.6 s; gauge 8.0 s | <= 120 s | VS-B |
| 3 | Bus overload PWR-OVL | breaker lamp (never a seam's own breaker, SN-15 L-C), cable gauge | cable scorch, arc POP, `served_pm` lamp brightness down the branch, 480 px = 240 ticks = 4.0 s | yes | walk | another fixed gauge on the bus 8.0 s; scanner 2.6 s (no Suit key measures POWER) | <= 120 s | VS-B |
| 4 | Broken breaker | breaker lamp shows closed (never a seam's own breaker, SN-15 L-C) | dark branch fixtures, relay clunk, 384 px = 192 ticks = 3.2 s | yes (the dark is the cue) | walk | gauge 8.0 s; scanner 2.6 s | <= 120 s | VS-B |
| 5 | Coolant leak CLT-LEAK | coolant gauge, radiator glow | frost fan, DRIP, cavitation TICK, 384 px = 3.2 s | yes | walk | scanner 2.6 s (single reservoir on the slice); gauge 8.0 s where a second exists | <= 120 s | VS-B |
| 6 | Pump stall (STARVE) | pump sprite shown running | dark branch fixtures, no cavitation TICK, beam sweep, 240 px = 2.0 s | yes | walk | scanner 2.6 s | <= 120 s | VS-B |
| 7 | Fuel flange drip | sight-glass | PUDDLE plus DRIP (glossy, flat, ember edge), 144 px = 72 ticks = 1.2 s | yes | walk | scanner 2.6 s (one tank sight-glass on the slice) | <= 120 s | VS-B |
| 8 | Hull fatigue toward breach | hull gauge | the groan TICK with `since_steps`, hairlines along ribs; listen for the next groan, up to the Graph's groan cadence (Graph-owned, not valued here); 0 s of walk | no (hear) | none | another fixed hull gauge 8.0 s; scanner 2.6 s (`suit.integrity` measures the suit and does not count) | <= 120 s | VS-B |

Scan shortcut (every fault): lit channel, tool slot 3 `tool_scanner`, the noise class is Interaction's (not yet declared, OQ-SN-13), 2.6 s lower bound. **Per-class rows (deferred classes):**

| Class | Rung 1 | Rung 2 | Rung 3 | Rung 4 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| (a) phantom | look at the floor for footprints (M17) and wait for an Attention tell that does not come: 2 to 5 s, lit | walk through it (it neither pushes nor blocks): risk, 1 to 3 s | a saner crewmate, VS-B | `PHANTOM_LIFE_S` 8 to 20 s |
| (a) hidden real entity (HIDE) | its footprints and its own sounds remain: 0 extra, listen | a second view from another position | VS-B | `HIDE_LIFE_S` 8 to 20 s |
| (c) crewmates as horrors | the true voice and the true beam colour: 0 s | a second look from another position *(a crewmate has no instrument, OQ-SN-8)* | ask a saner crewmate | leave eligibility (`level` above `SAN_HARM_EXIT`): minutes, an escort home |
| (d) phantom salvage | carry it and listen for the weight breath-step (Interaction's VS-A `check_kind` HEFT, IA-30): pickup time only (drop stowed HEAVY first) | SCAN 2.6 s lower bound | PALETTE, VS-A | the trip home (the dearest) |

**Class (d) design test.** `C_check <= 0.05 x T_home` where `T_home` is the one-way trip home with the item, **from the pickup to the home install point** (the route to the pod console, the hop, and the home carry to the HARDPOINT or stow point; Boarding's reading (c), ASK-BS-N1), because the trip home is the dearest rung only when the item's worth is learned at the point of use. **Example:** `T_home` 120 s, SCAN 2.6 s: ratio 0.022, passes; `T_home` 40 s: 0.065 fails, which means an item that near is not worth a trip and the check is not the point. Boarding's finding C-BS-11 holds: a SCAN passes the test on no MVP route (0.076 for `htr_a` under this reading); it is not a defect at the MVP because class (d) ships none, and it binds VS-A junk placement (`T_home >= 20 x C_check`, Derelict Authoring's to place). `T_EQUIP` stays 0 as the carried lower bound until Suit registers a swap duration (OQ-SN-6).

#### D.8 `ambient_floor_invariant`

The `ambient_floor_invariant` formula is defined as:

`min over ring of AMBIENT_FLOOR[ring] >= SAN_TELL_OFF + AMBIENT_MARGIN` (a); `|f - t| >= STAGE_HYST` for every floor `f` and every stage threshold `t` in {`STAGE_ENTER[s]`, `STAGE_ENTER[s] + STAGE_HYST`} (b); `AMBIENT_FLOOR[ring + 1] <= AMBIENT_FLOOR[ring]` (c); `max over ring of RING_EXPOSURE[ring] < REC_SETTLED` (d); `max over ring of RING_EXPOSURE[ring] + ABOARD_EXPOSURE - REC_IDLE < DRIFT_MAX_PER_RUN` (e)

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `AMBIENT_FLOOR[ring]` | int mSU | 62000 / 52000 / 43000 | knob | level below which the ambient term stops |
| `AMBIENT_MARGIN` | int mSU | 5000 (safe 3000 to 10000) | knob | clearance above `SAN_TELL_OFF` |
| `STAGE_HYST`, `STAGE_ENTER[s]` | int mSU | 2000; 70000 / 45000 / 25000 | knobs | stage geometry |
| `RING_EXPOSURE`, `REC_SETTLED`, `ABOARD_EXPOSURE`, `REC_IDLE` | int mSU/run | 0 / 4 / 8; 12; 20; 1 | knobs | the home-refuge and sub-cap relations |

**Output Range:** pass or fail per clause, evaluated at boot and on every retune; a missing floor for any ring refuses boot with a closed `SANITY_BOOT_*` reason, and no default of 0 exists. At the defaults: (a) 43000 >= 40000, margin 3000; (b) the nearest pairs are 43000 to 45000 (2000, exactly `STAGE_HYST`), 52000 to 47000 (5000), 62000 to 70000 (8000), so it passes with the ring-3 floor sitting exactly on the clearance; (c) 62000 >= 52000 >= 43000; (d) 8 < 12; (e) 8 + 20 - 1 = 27 < 36. Calm waiting therefore cannot reach `tell_on`, stage 3 or `harm_eligible`. **Stage at each floor:** ring 1 stage 1; ring 2 stage 1 (5000 above the stage-2 exit 47000); ring 3 stage 2 (the level sawtooths within 26 mSU below 43000; stage 2 holds because its exit is 47000).
**Example (time from `SAN_MAX`, net = `RING_EXPOSURE + ABOARD_EXPOSURE - REC_IDLE`):** ring 1, net 19: stage 1 after `ceil(30000 / 19)` = 1579 runs = 315.8 s (5.3 min), floor 62000 after 2000 runs = 400 s (6.7 min). Ring 2, net 23: stage 1 after 1305 runs = 261.0 s, floor 52000 after 2087 runs = 417.4 s. Ring 3, net 27: stage 1 after 1112 runs = 222.4 s, stage 2 after 2038 runs = 407.6 s, floor 43000 after 2112 runs = 422.4 s. At the floor the level sawtooths: one run above it loses the ring's aboard rate (19 / 23 / 27 for ring 1 / 2 / 3), then recovers 1 per run, so it stays within `[floor - 18, floor + 1]` in ring 1, `[floor - 22, floor + 1]` in ring 2 and `[floor - 26, floor + 1]` in ring 3. **Hangover:** a seat that left the ring-1 floor (62000, stage 1) and settles at home needs `ceil(10000 / 12)` = 834 runs = 166.8 s to cross the stage-1 exit at 72000; from the ring-3 floor, 4000 / 12 = 334 runs (66.8 s) clears stage 2 and 29000 / 12 = 2417 runs (483.4 s) clears stage 1 (both figures assume the home body is in ring 1, the MVP baseline WM-27; a home in ring 3 nets `REC_SETTLED - RING_EXPOSURE[3]` = 4 per run above its floor, so the same climb takes three times as long).

#### D.9 `harm_hysteresis_margin`

The `harm_hysteresis_margin` formula is defined as:

`entry_edge_margin = SAN_HARM_EXIT - SAN_HARM_ENTER`; assertion `entry_edge_margin >= ceil(HARM_CHANNEL_TICKS / SANITY_DRIFT_DIVISOR) x max(DRIFT_MAX_PER_RUN, REC_MAX)`; `exit_edge_window = ceil(HARM_CHANNEL_TICKS / SANITY_DRIFT_DIVISOR) x REC_MAX`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `HARM_CHANNEL_TICKS` | int ticks | 120 (cited) | Interaction | length of a harm channel |
| `SAN_HARM_ENTER`, `SAN_HARM_EXIT` | int mSU | 15000, 20000 | knobs | gate band |
| `DRIFT_MAX_PER_RUN`, `REC_MAX` | int mSU/run | 36, 15 | knobs | the fastest the level can move per run in each direction |

**Output Range:** the margin must be at least 360 (10 runs x 36); the default 5000 passes at 13.9x. The safe range for `SAN_HARM_EXIT` follows: 15360 and up (the formula's minimum) and, to keep an eligible actor from staying eligible for minutes, at most 25000. The **exit edge** is different: an actor who starts a channel while recovering within `exit_edge_window` = 10 x 15 = 150 mSU below `SAN_HARM_EXIT` can be pushed over it mid-channel, which is Interaction's `ABORT(NOT_ELIGIBLE)` (AC-IA-88); the entry edge cannot flip from drift alone.
**Example:** an eligible actor settling at home at `level` 19900 starts a channel: recovery 12 per run for 10 runs moves the level to 20020, above 20000, so the bit clears at the run that crosses and the channel aborts at the next `LIT_RECHECK_TICKS` check. Starting at 19800 the level ends at 19920 and the channel completes.

#### D.10 `horror_encounter_budget` *(E3; provisional on Horror)*

The `horror_encounter_budget` formula is defined as:

`pay_h = min(EXP_HORROR, HORROR_ENC_BUDGET - spent_h)` and `spent_h' = spent_h + pay_h` for each near horror `h` each run; `spent_h' = 0` after `HORROR_ENC_RESET_S x 5` consecutive runs with `h` out of range; `min_encounters(a, b) = ceil((a - b) / HORROR_ENC_BUDGET)`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `EXP_HORROR` | int mSU/run | 20 (safe 0 to 28) | knob | rate while a registered HORROR is within `HORROR_NEAR_R` |
| `HORROR_ENC_BUDGET` | int mSU | 3000 (safe 1500 to 6000) | knob | most one horror can charge in one encounter |
| `HORROR_ENC_RESET_S` | int s | 120 (safe 30 to 300) | knob | out-of-range time that ends an encounter |
| `HORROR_NEAR_R` | int px | 288 (safe 144 to 480) | knob | distance from the seat's foot origin to the horror's `pos_px`; no light condition |
| `HORROR_TRACK_MAX` | int | 4 | knob | tracked refs; a fifth evicts the stalest encounter |
| `spent_h` | int mSU | 0 to `HORROR_ENC_BUDGET` | `sanity.ledger` `horror_enc[]` | spend in the current encounter |

**Output Range:** `pay_h` in 0 to 20; one horror charges at most 3000 mSU per encounter, reached after 150 runs = 30 s of contact. **Farm bound:** reaching `SAN_HARM_ENTER` from stage-1 entry (55000 mSU) by one horror alone takes `55000 / 3000` = 18.3, so at least 19 whole encounters, each separated by 120 s out of range, and `REC_IDLE` repays 600 mSU per gap, so the true count is `55000 / (2850 - 600)` = 24.4 (contact nets `EXP_HORROR - REC_IDLE` = 19 per run, 2850 per encounter, against 600 repaid per gap), about 25 encounters and over an hour.
**Example:** a repelled horror held 250 px away: runs 1 to 150 pay 20 each (3000), run 151 onward pays `min(20, 0)` = 0; after the horror is out of range for 600 runs the budget resets to 3000 available.

#### D.11 `lie_rate_estimate` *(design-time calibration aid; not a second onset rule)*

The `lie_rate_estimate` formula is defined as:

`T_on = G + 1 / (lambda_e x p)`; `n10 = 600 / T_on`; `N_avg = min(LIES_MAX[s], L_avg / T_on)`; inverse `ONSET_PM = round(1000 / (lambda_e x (T_target - G)))`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `lambda_e` | float per s | 0.1 reference (safe 0.03 to 0.3) | reference: 4 eligible surfaces per compartment, one compartment entry per 40 s; replaced by the Debug Overlay's measured `eligible_entries_per_min` | gated-event arrival rate for one seat |
| `p` | float | `ONSET_PM[s] / 1000` | knob | accept probability |
| `G` | int s | `LIE_GAP_S[s]` 45 / 25 / 10 | knob | dead time after an onset |
| `L_avg` | float s | 80 | mean of the 40 to 120 life band | mean lie life |
| `T_target` | float s | 200 / 70 / 35 for stages 1 to 3 | playtest alternative | target mean time between onsets |

**Output Range:** `T_on` above `G`; `n10` the onsets per 10 minutes; `N_avg` the mean number of live lies by Little's law, clamped to `LIES_MAX[s]`. At the default `ONSET_PM`: stage 1 `T_on` = 45 + 66.7 = 111.7 s, `n10` 5.4, `N_avg` 0.72; stage 2 58.3 s, 10.3, 1.37; stage 3 28.2 s, 21.3, 2.84 (cap 4). The inverse gives the playtest alternative for a gentler early curve: `ONSET_PM` 65 / 222 / 400 for `T_target` 200 / 70 / 35 s (stage 1: 1000 / (0.1 x 155) = 64.5; stage 2: 1000 / (0.1 x 45) = 222.2; stage 3: 1000 / (0.1 x 25) = 400). *(Orchestrator ruling: the defaults stay the rulings' 150 / 300 / 550 and this formula is a calibration aid. The stage-1 default gives a lie live about 72 percent of the time on the reference rate, which is more than "subtle, gauge-only" suggests; the alternative is offered as a knob range and the decision rests with the first playtest. The result depends on `lambda_e` more than on `ONSET_PM`, so the measured counter replaces the reference.)*
**Example:** measured `lambda_e` 0.05 per s at stage 2 with `ONSET_PM` 300: `T_on` = 25 + 1 / (0.05 x 0.3) = 25 + 66.7 = 91.7 s, `n10` 6.5, `N_avg` 0.87.

#### D.12 `immunity_sweep_ratio` *(design-time; the check on `DEBUNK_IMMUNE_S`)*

The `immunity_sweep_ratio` formula is defined as:

`SD = N_s x (t_check + t_move) / min(DEBUNK_IMMUNE_S, t_dwell)`; a sweep is dominant when `SD < 0.25` at stage 2 or deeper

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `N_s` | int | 1+ | Derelict Authoring / slice content | lie-eligible surfaces in the compartment |
| `t_check` | float s | 2 | `FIELD_CHECK_TICKS` 120 / `TICK_RATE` | one scan |
| `t_move` | float s | 1.5 | design assumption (reach is 0.6 s of walk; 1.5 s to reposition) | between scans |
| `DEBUNK_IMMUNE_S` | int s | 60 (safe 45 to 120) | knob | immunity window; assertion `DEBUNK_IMMUNE_S >= max(LIE_GAP_S)` (60 >= 45) |
| `t_dwell` | float s | 1+ | the player's plan | how long the seat intends to stay |

**Output Range:** `SD` above 0; below 0.25 the sweep costs less than a quarter of the stay and "scan everything on entry" is a free habit. **Example at `DEBUNK_IMMUNE_S` 60:** `N_s` 4, dwell 40 s: `4 x 3.5 / 40` = 0.35 (not dominant); `N_s` 2, dwell 40: 0.175 (dominant, hence the advisory 3-surface authoring floor, OQ-SN-11); `N_s` 4, dwell 120: `14 / 60` = 0.233 (marginally dominant, a legitimate purchase for a long task). At 120 s the third case would be 0.117, which is why 60 is the default.

#### D.13 `phantom_response_bound` *(class a; design-time; [UNV] Attention units)*

The `phantom_response_bound` formula is defined as:

`extra_noise = response_ticks x (NOISE_PER_TICK_run - NOISE_PER_TICK_walk)`; design bound `extra_noise <= PHANTOM_RESPONSE_MAX_PM x ATT_THRESHOLD / 1000` with `PHANTOM_RESPONSE_MAX_PM` 250

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `response_ticks` | int ticks | 0 to `PHANTOM_LIFE_S` x `TICK_RATE` | the player | ticks spent running from a phantom |
| `NOISE_PER_TICK` run, walk | int AU | 4, 1 (cited) | Attention registry | noise per tick |
| `ATT_THRESHOLD` | int AU | 6000 (cited) | Attention registry | the breach threshold |
| `PHANTOM_RESPONSE_MAX_PM` | int permille | 250 | design bound, not a knob | share of the threshold one encounter may cost |

**Output Range:** raw noise units, before Attention's decay `TAU_ATT_TICKS` (not re-derived here, **[UNV]**). **Example:** a typical 6 s sprint: `360 x 3` = 1080 AU <= `250 x 6000 / 1000` = 1500, passes. A sprint for a phantom's full 20 s life: `1200 x 3` = 3600 AU, 60 percent of the threshold before decay, which fails the bound; the escape is Attention's decay and `PHANTOM_GAP_S` 60 s, and the question is carried in Open Questions (the stage-3 `PHANTOM_LIFE_S` upper end may need to drop to 12 s: `720 x 3` = 2160, still over; the bound is a playtest target, not a proof).

## Edge Cases

Format: **If [condition]**: [exact outcome]. [rationale]. Each bullet ends with its id `EC-SN-n`, which Acceptance Criteria cite. Every outcome below is a defined result of the rules above, never "handle gracefully".

#### Zero, maximum and out-of-range values

- **If `level` is exactly on a stage threshold (70000, 45000 or 25000 on entry; 72000, 47000 or 27000 on exit)**: the current stage holds, because entry is strictly below and exit strictly above (D.2). Equality must not flip a stage, or a rate that lands on a threshold would chatter. *(EC-SN-1)*
- **If `level` reaches 0**: it clamps at 0 (D.1 `clamp(level - net, 0, SAN_MAX)`); the stage stays 3, `tell_on` stays set and `harm_eligible` is set once the dwell has passed; exposure keeps being computed but cannot lower it further, and recovery lifts it as usual. Nothing happens at zero: there is no breakdown, no death, no new state (Pillar 1: the world, not a threshold, is the consequence). *(EC-SN-2)*
- **If `level` is `SAN_MAX` and `net` is negative (a settled home at full sanity)**: it clamps at `SAN_MAX`; the recovery is wasted. At stage 0 the onset path is not entered, which costs nothing (the pre-draw gate "stage at least 1" fails before any draw). *(EC-SN-3)*
- **If a second or third startle arrives while `pending` is full**: `pending'` is `min(PENDING_MAX, pending - draw + startle_add)`, so 1500 + 1500 = 3000 fits exactly and a third 1500 is discarded entirely. Overflow only ever reduces drift, the safe direction; the debt is bounded at two stacked startles, about 177 runs (35.4 s) of extra descent after a BREACH, aboard in ring 1 at CALM above the floor (headroom 36 - 19 = 17; 3000 / 17 rounds up to 177). *(EC-SN-4)*
- **If a restore finds a stored value out of range (a `level` above `SAN_MAX`, a negative counter, a `stage` of 4, an unknown lie `kind`, a ledger `end_run` before `start_run`)**: the restore of that block fails with a closed `SANITY_RESTORE_*` reason; the value is never clamped (SL-15). A clamped value would let a corrupt save quietly change the player's sanity and would break `state_hash` equality (G4). *(EC-SN-5)*
- **If `ring_of(actor_section(actor))` is unknown or outside 1 to 3 (a section Web Map has not resolved, or a future fourth ring)**: the seat uses ring 1 and Sanity logs it once per seat (the AT-6 fallback). That is the shallowest, least-draining reading; the real depth data is Web Map's (WM-27 publishes it; baseline ring 1) *(provisional — Web Map & Transit In Design, draft on disk)*, and the boot lint refuses a ring with no `AMBIENT_FLOOR`. *(EC-SN-6)*
- **If `AMBIENT_FLOOR` is missing for any ring in use**: boot refuses with a closed `SANITY_BOOT_*` reason. A silent default floor of 0 would remove the ambient bound and let calm waiting reach `SAN_HARM_ENTER` (SN-29). *(EC-SN-7)*
- **If `E3` is computed with five horrors near**: only `HORROR_TRACK_MAX` 4 encounters are tracked; the stalest is evicted and the fifth is paid from a fresh budget. `E` can then reach 100 or more, but `net` is clamped to `DRIFT_MAX_PER_RUN` 36, so the lead invariants hold. The count is a design absurdity, not a hazard. *(EC-SN-8)*
- **If a tuning change makes `DRIFT_MAX_PER_RUN` 40 with nothing else changed**: D.4 (i) and (iii) fail ((ii) passes at 75 s) and boot refuses (`SANITY_BOOT_LEAD_INVARIANT`). The lead invariants are build assertions, not guidance. *(EC-SN-9)*

#### Simultaneous rules and ordering inside one run

- **If two stage thresholds would both be crossed in one run**: impossible under the cap (`25 x 36` = 900 mSU per dwell against gaps of at least 20000). It can occur only through a restore or a debug force, where the stored stage is kept after a restore and a forced recompute moves directly to the target with the dwell saturated (SN-4). One transition per run, the deeper applying if two dwell timers expire together. *(EC-SN-10)*
- **If BREACH is entered on the same run as a startle and the cap binds**: `E2` (28) applies that run, the 1500 goes into `pending` and drains only from later headroom (D.1 example E). Startle never bypasses the cap, so the lead invariants stay true build assertions. *(EC-SN-11)*
- **If a `field_check` completes on the same tick as the lie's `end_run`**: expiry is evaluated first (SN-17), the lie ends silently, and the check then records immunity on a non-lying subject. Which came first is not observable to the player: both end with no mark. *(EC-SN-12)*
- **If a repair or a falsification completes in the same tick that the lie's witness ends**: Interaction's apply runs before the `sanity.drift` hook in the tick, so the lie is already DEBUNKED and the immunity is stamped before the hook would have INVALIDATED it. Immunity on a subject whose witness has gone is harmless and uniform. *(EC-SN-13)*
- **If two seats gain a lit-entry event on the same run**: seats are processed in ascending PlayerId, each with its own streams and counters, so neither seat's draws depend on the other's; only the order of work differs. *(EC-SN-14)*
- **If a stage change and a lie activation land on the same run**: stage first (SN-2 order), so the activation re-check and the cap use the new stage's `LIES_MAX`; an existing lie is never ended by the change (SN-16). *(EC-SN-15)*
- **If a real BREACH or a real TELL begins while a FALSE_OK lie is live on the same loop**: the real Attention outputs are untouched (AT-17). If the seat's compartment atmosphere band leaves OK the lie is INVALIDATED at the next run (L-G); if the fault is elsewhere the lie continues and the seat has the tell, the Suit cues and the witness to read. A lie never delays, replaces or fakes the tell. *(EC-SN-16)*
- **If a harm channel's gate flips mid-channel**: Interaction ABORTs with `NOT_ELIGIBLE` at the next `LIT_RECHECK_TICKS` check (AC-IA-88). It can arise from drift only at the exit edge and only within `exit_edge_window` 150 mSU of `SAN_HARM_EXIT` while the actor recovers (D.9); QA reaches it by settling an eligible actor at home. The entry edge cannot flip from drift. *(EC-SN-17)*

#### Degenerate strategies

- **If the player stands in a calm derelict to lower their own sanity (a self-harm lever)**: the ambient term stops at `AMBIENT_FLOOR[ring]` (62000 / 52000 / 43000), all above `SAN_TELL_OFF` + `AMBIENT_MARGIN` (D.8 (a)), so waiting can reach stage 1 or at most stage 2 in ring 3 and can never set `tell_on`, enter stage 3 or reach `harm_eligible`. Only Attention events and real horror contact lower it further, and those are the world's. *(EC-SN-18)*
- **If the player holds a repelled horror at the edge of `HORROR_NEAR_R` to farm exposure**: the encounter budget caps one horror at `HORROR_ENC_BUDGET` 3000 mSU per encounter (30 s of contact), and the encounter resets only after 120 s out of range, during which `REC_IDLE` repays 600 (D.10). The farm costs more than an hour for about 25 encounters, and the cap makes it no faster than any other descent. *(EC-SN-19)*
- **If the player scans every eligible surface on entering a compartment**: each scan costs `FIELD_CHECK_TICKS` 120 (lit, reach, Interaction's noise class) and immunity lasts `DEBUNK_IMMUNE_S` 60 s uniformly, so a sweep of 4 surfaces for a 40 s stay has `SD` 0.35 and is not dominant (D.12). With 2 or fewer eligible surfaces it is dominant; that is the advisory authoring floor (OQ-SN-11), not a Sanity mutator. *(EC-SN-20)*
- **If the player turns the lamp off and on, or sweeps the beam, to spam lit-entry events**: a change in `lit` caused only by the seat's own lamp inside the same compartment is not an event, and each subject has `LIT_ENTRY_DEBOUNCE_S` 20 s. Re-entering the compartment re-sets `entry_pending` and re-fires the batch, but `LIE_GAP_S`, `LIES_MAX` and the debounce gate it, and a gated-out event draws nothing. *(EC-SN-21)*
- **If the player stays unlit for longer than any timer and then lights up in a compartment with live gauges**: `entry_pending` has persisted since entry, so the batch fires on the first run `C(P)` is non-empty. There is no free dodge by waiting in the dark. Staying unlit gives zero onsets, and gives up the lit-only verbs and the readings (SN-16); it carries no sanity cost (SN-6). *(EC-SN-22)*
- **If the player quits and reloads, or dies and reloads, to reroll a lie**: the ledger, the per-subject visit counters, `lit_prev` and `entry_pending` restore exactly, so the same actions replay the same draws (SN-24). No `restore_reason` changes content. The residual is that identical actions after a death replay the same lie at the same moment, priced by the death. *(EC-SN-23)*
- **If the player burns draws in one room to change what happens in another**: it cannot; the lie stream is keyed `"sanity.lie/<PlayerId>/<stable_id>"` per subject, so only gated events on that subject advance its counter (CQ2). The only way to change a subject's dice is to visit the subject. *(EC-SN-24)*
- **If the player repairs a subject repeatedly to farm the "came undone" weight**: the weight doubles `ONSET_PM` for `REPAIR_BIAS_S` 60 s, but each onset still passes `LIE_GAP_S`, `LIES_MAX`, the severity and witness rules, and L-I immunity after any check, so the loop yields at most one extra lie per gap. A lie on a subject the player just repaired is the intended shape of the fiction. *(EC-SN-25)*
- **If an actor causes harm hoping to be rewarded or to lower a witness's sanity**: `record_harm_event` changes nothing but the `incidents` ring (no level change, no reward, no recovery, no E4 in the MVP; SN-13). Harm is a story the web told, not a lever. *(EC-SN-26)*

#### Save, restore and replay

- **If a restore lands mid-dwell (`harm_below_runs` or `stage_dwell` partly counted)**: the persisted counters resume and the bit does not reset; the restore never recomputes the stage (SN-4, SN-11). *(EC-SN-27)*
- **If a snapshot is taken between hook runs**: `lit_prev`, `entry_pending`, the debounce stamps and the visit counters are persisted ledger state and are not rebuilt from the world, so an uninterrupted run and a restored run hold the same set at their next run and `state_hash` agrees (G4; SN-1). *(EC-SN-28)*
- **If a restore lands while a lie is PENDING**: the entry keeps its `start_run`, `end_run` and activation delay and activates on schedule after its SN-15 re-check; nothing is re-drawn (no counter moves in `post_restore`). *(EC-SN-29)*
- **If the ledger holds a `subject_ref` that no longer resolves after the Graph and Layout blocks have restored**: a hard `SANITY_RESTORE_*` failure, never a silent drop. A subject destroyed before the snapshot was INVALIDATED and pruned in the same run, so an unresolved ref means corruption. *(EC-SN-30)*
- **If a death stub reloads the last autosave at stage 3**: the level, stage, ledger and counters come back from the autosave (at most `AUTOSAVE_INTERVAL_TICKS` of progress lost); death is not a cure (SL H3), and a seat already low wakes still low. *(EC-SN-31)*
- **If a save carries a `last_tell_epoch` equal to the live `tell_epoch` and `tell_outcome` BREACHED**: no startle is added on the first run after load; the epoch was already credited (SN-7). A restore never double-credits and never drops the pending debt. *(EC-SN-32)*
- **If a save has a `schema_version` the loader does not support**: the block refuses with the SL-15 closed reason; Sanity ships `supported_versions` [1] and no migration, so a newer file is refused rather than guessed. *(EC-SN-33)*
- **If `post_restore` runs**: `sanity.perception` is rebuilt in full from `sanity.state` and `sanity.ledger`, so the first RUNNING frame already carries the right lies (SN-23); a one-frame unfiltered view would be a free oracle. *(EC-SN-34)*

#### Hallucination interplay

- **If the seat leaves the compartment mid-lie**: the lie's clock keeps running in sim time and nothing renders; on return an unexpired lie is drawn again from its remaining life (SN-16). Leaving for `LIE_LIFE_S` clears it, which is the time rung with a trip attached. *(EC-SN-35)*
- **If the lying subject is destroyed or salvaged mid-lie**: the entry is INVALIDATED at the next run, with no immunity (a destroyed subject cannot be immune) and no EXPIRED stamp; `visit[p][u]` is pruned. *(EC-SN-36)*
- **If the witness ends mid-lie (the fault was repaired)**: a FALSE_OK lie is INVALIDATED at the next run, because L-W no longer holds; a FALSE_FAIL lie ends when the claimed failure is now true. In both cases the true state equals the displayed state or the witness is gone. *(EC-SN-37)*
- **If the true state becomes equal to the displayed state mid-lie (a real fault arises on a subject shown broken)**: INVALIDATED silently; a lie that says what is true is not a lie and leaves no leak to explain. *(EC-SN-38)*
- **If `field_check` targets a subject with no lie**: `record_falsification` stamps the uniform immunity, nothing visible changes and the level is unchanged (SN-17); a clean check leaks nothing. *(EC-SN-39)*
- **If two seats look at the same gauge at different stages**: each seat has an independent ledger entry; seat A may see a lie where seat B sees the truth, with no shared state. This is what makes the saner-crewmate rung work (VS-B). *(EC-SN-40)*
- **If a subject is eligible for both FALSE_OK and FALSE_FAIL**: the kind comes from the subject's true state (degraded or failed gives FALSE_OK; healthy gives FALSE_FAIL), then GLIDE and SPRITE_SWAP are drawn by `KIND_W` where reach allows. *(EC-SN-41)*
- **If every instrument on a loop would be lying at once**: the lie is ineligible (L-2I); in the MVP the scanner is honest, so a single-instrument loop (the fuel sight-glass, the reservoir) is lie-eligible only because the scanner counts. *(EC-SN-42)*
- **If the stage falls while more lies are live than the new stage's `LIES_MAX` allows**: no lie is removed; no new onset occurs until the live count is below the new cap, and the excess runs to expiry (a vanishing lie at a boundary would be a tell of the boundary). *(EC-SN-43)*
- **If a surface is itself the witness of another lie**: it is never a lying surface (a witness is a consequence-channel item); the candidate is skipped without a draw. *(EC-SN-44)*
- **If the first lie of a world has no fault-free lit subject to land on**: the onset is deferred (SN-16): no lie of any kind is raised and `lies_seen` stays 0 until a fault-free lit subject exists (the home ship is the expected place). *(EC-SN-45)*
- **If class (b) is switched off at runtime (`CLASSES_ENABLED`)**: live lies expire normally and no new onsets occur; no snapshot format changes. *(EC-SN-46)*
- **If a phantom spawns while Attention is CALM (class a, deferred)**: `attention.view`, `TellAudit` and `emitters()` are unchanged (AC-AT-73); a phantom never creates a tell or a calm. *(EC-SN-47)*
- **If a hidden real entity (HIDE) enters `HIDE_SAFE_R`, its pre-act sub-tell, BREACH or SUBSIDING-in-locus**: the HIDE collapses that run and the next frame draws the body; its footprints were never hidden. *(EC-SN-48)*

#### Seat lifecycle and co-op hooks

- **If a seat is DOWNED**: it keeps drifting and keeps onset (exposure is real while down); a DEAD seat freezes, and in the MVP the death stub replaces the frozen state with the restored one (SN-3). *(EC-SN-49)*
- **If a seat unbinds and rebinds (VS-B)**: the stored state under its PlayerId is kept, with no drift while unbound; there is no join-to-reset lever. A PlayerId with no stored state starts at `SAN_MAX`. *(EC-SN-50)*
- **If two seats are both `harm_eligible` (VS-B)**: each is drawn as a horror to the other, each has its own victim tell, and either may start a lit channel; the first to complete kills and `record_harm_event` logs it. The gate never reads the target's level, only the attacker's bit. *(EC-SN-51)*
- **If an eligible actor has no valid target (the single-seat MVP)**: the bit is computed and queryable but nothing happens; class (c) rendering is disabled by `CLASSES_ENABLED` and exercised with stub actors. *(EC-SN-52)*
- **If a damaged home body has no compartment with all bands OK**: `settled` is false everywhere, recovery is `REC_IDLE` 1, and the level does not recover: a ring-1 home has E1 0, so the level still climbs by 1 per run toward `SAN_MAX`; a ring-2 home falls at +3 per run to the floor and sawtooths within `[52000 - 2, 52001]`; a ring-3 home falls at +7 and sawtooths within `[43000 - 6, 43001]`, so the level hovers at its ring's floor only in rings 2 and 3; the cure is repairing one compartment to all-OK bands (SN-8). The worst ambient state of a damaged home is its ring's floor, never lower without Attention or horror events. *(EC-SN-53)*
- **If `REC_HOME_BASE` is proposed again**: it is not adopted (CQ3); a playtest signal that a damaged home is a sanity trap is the reopen condition. *(EC-SN-54)*
- **If debug `force_level` crosses a threshold**: the stage and bit update through the same D.2 and D.3 path with the dwell saturated, the ledger is untouched, and the cap invariants are bypassed (SN-9 debug exception); this is never a player surface. *(EC-SN-55)*
- **If `lit_static(ref)` is not published by Emitter State**: only the compartment-entry batch (a) fires; world-caused lit changes while the seat stays in the compartment produce no event (SN-16), which only lowers the lie count. *(EC-SN-56)*
- **If no node-to-subject mapping exists for a gauge (nobody registered it as an OBJECT subject)**: `lit(ref)` answers like unlit (EM-21), so that gauge is never a candidate; the lie set shrinks, and the authoring lint is routed to Interaction and Derelict Authoring. *(EC-SN-57)*
- **If the seat is IN_POD mid-leg**: E1 = `RING_EXPOSURE[held_ring]` with no aboard term, recovery is `REC_IDLE`, no lit-entry event fires in the pod (the pod has no lie-eligible module-body subject), and live lies keep their sim-time clocks (SN-16). *(EC-SN-58)*
- **If a hidden horror gouges a duct or cable (a WORN descriptor, not a trail)**: that descriptor is excluded from the candidate set and any live lie on it is INVALIDATED at the next run; the gouge reads true to every seat (SN-15 L-C). *(EC-SN-59)*

## Dependencies

Status vocabulary: **designed** (GDD on disk, header "Designed"); **in flight** (GDD on disk as a draft, `Status: In Design`); **undesigned-provisional** (no file; this GDD is the first writer of its half). Type is the strength of the dependency for this system; "Counterpart row" says whether the neighbour's own GDD already lists Sanity (design-docs rule: dependencies must be bidirectional).

**Soft cycle Sanity to Perception Filter (IDX:267).** "Sanity decides what is perceived; the Filter draws it. Resolution: Sanity is authoritative and writes only perception state; the Filter reads that state and writes only CanvasItem/Material/Light2D properties ... The Filter never calls back." Adopted: no Sanity symbol reads a Filter value, and the Filter holds no handle on `sanity.state` or `sanity.ledger`. The Filter's own write set is narrower than the index quote: `CanvasItem` and material properties only, with decoys drawn by Lighting (LO-21).

**Index corrections owed (routed, not edited here).** The Graph to Sanity edge is missing (soft, LSG:867); Interaction to Sanity is Hard and missing in the index's Interaction row (IA:824); the Sanity to Save/Load edge is a design-time contract and not a runtime call (SL OQ-15); the Attention row in the index should read `phase`, `tier`, `tell_outcome` and `tell_epoch`, not `level_pm` (C11); the index description of the kernel row ("commands") is refreshed in SN-17 (C13).

**Cycle check.** Sanity depends on Attention, Interaction and Suit; none of them reads any Sanity value except Interaction's gate query, which reads the SIM bit (never `PERCEPTION_OUT`). Attention reads nothing of Sanity (one-way, SN-7). There is no cycle in the simulation graph; the only loop in the design (Attention to sanity to lies to player noise to Attention) passes through the human.

## Tuning Knobs

Every value is a `[PLACEHOLDER]` registered as such unless the row says **cited** or **relation** (reflexion P-1); tuned in `assets/data/sanity/` (gameplay values are data-driven, never hardcoded). A ★ marks a **registry candidate**: a cross-system fact another GDD reads, gates on, or must stay consistent with. Unstarred knobs are Sanity-internal and are registered as placeholders but are not facts other systems cite. **Retune rule (P-2, P-7):** any change to a stage threshold, a harm or tell threshold, `DRIFT_MAX_PER_RUN`, an exposure rate, the divisor, or a floor re-runs D.4, D.8 and D.9 in full before it is accepted. The D.4 margins are thin (2.4 s on bound (i), 5.6 s on bound (iii)) and cap 36 is the largest passing value, so a knob that raises the descent rate or moves a threshold up is a knob that can fail a lead invariant. Safe ranges below are computed **with every other knob at its default**. Columns follow the template: too-high and too-low symptoms are what a tester sees, not abstractions.

#### Level, stages and the two flags

| Knob | Default | Unit | Safe range | Too high | Too low | Interacts with |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `SAN_MAX` | 100000 | mSU | fixed unit scale | integer overflow is not a concern below 2^53, but every threshold must be rescaled with it | resolution falls below the slowest rate (1 mSU per run) | every threshold scales with it; never reaches a player surface (IA-39) |
| `STAGE_ENTER[1..3]` ★ | 70000 / 45000 / 25000 | mSU | stage 1: 69600 to 80000; stage 2 and 3 each at least `2 x STAGE_HYST` from neighbours | lies from the first minutes (stage 1 early) or stage 3 reachable without events | stage 1 unreachable in a session; D.4 (i) fails below 69600 | D.4 (i) to (iii); D.8 (b); the Filter's stage 0 to 3 fixtures (PIPE:904); Failure & Trail's stage ladder (FT:61-75) |
| `STAGE_HYST` | 2000 | mSU | 1000 to 4000 | sticky stages: a recovered seat keeps its lies | stage chatter at a boundary (a lie-vocabulary flicker, a PR-13 risk) | D.2; D.8 (b) clearance of every floor |
| `STAGE_DWELL_RUNS` | 25 | runs (5 s) | 10 to 50 | the stage lags the world, lies arrive late | stage flicker | the Filter's onset smoothing and PR-13 (at most 3 flashes in a rolling 1 s) |
| `SAN_TELL_ON` ★ | 30000 | mSU | 26000 to 30400 (D.4 (ii) needs > 25764, (i) needs < 30436) | the victim's tell appears too late, lead (ii) shrinks | the tell shows during ordinary stage-2 play | D.4 (i) margin 2.4 s and (ii); the Filter's victim fringe; Audio's tell flag |
| `SAN_TELL_OFF` ★ | 35000 | mSU | `SAN_TELL_ON` + 2000 to 40000 | the flag lingers after recovery | flag flicker at the edge | D.8 (a): every floor must stay at least `AMBIENT_MARGIN` above it |
| `SAN_HARM_ENTER` ★ | 15000 | mSU | 8000 to 16000 (D.4 (iii) needs <= 16035, (ii) needs <= 19235) | the gate opens too early; (iii) fails | friendly harm effectively unreachable | D.4 (ii), (iii); Interaction's gate (IA-31) |
| `SAN_HARM_EXIT` ★ | 20000 | mSU | 15360 (the D.9 boot assertion) to 25000 (also <= 27000 so `harm_eligible` implies stage 3) | an eligible actor stays eligible for minutes after recovering | a natural mid-channel flip becomes possible (D.9) | D.9; AC-IA-88; the escort recovery time (173 s settled, 34.7 min at `REC_IDLE`) |
| `SAN_HARM_DWELL_RUNS` ★ | 150 | runs (30 s) | 50 to 300 | harm unlocks late; the victim tell lead grows | the gate flaps; (iv) lead shrinks | D.3; D.4 (iv); Interaction's prompt |
| `SANITY_DRIFT_DIVISOR` | 12 | ticks | **relation, not a knob**: equals the kernel divisor (KRN:54, KRN:202) | - | - | `TICK_RATE`; D.4 converts runs to seconds with it |

#### The cap, the lead invariants and the pending pool

| Knob | Default | Unit | Safe range | Too high | Too low | Interacts with |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `DRIFT_MAX_PER_RUN` ★ | 36 | mSU per run (180 per s) | 18 to 36 (36 is the largest value that passes D.4 (i) and (iii)) | friendly harm can unlock inside minutes; boot refuses at 37 | breaches barely move the level, the startle debt never drains | D.4 (i) 2.4 s and (iii) 5.6 s margins; `EXP_*`; IDX:280 playtest signal |
| `REC_MAX` | 15 (derived: `REC_SETTLED + REC_CREW`) | mSU per run | = `REC_SETTLED + REC_CREW` | harmless (the clamp is a ceiling) | silently throttles co-op recovery | D.1 lower clamp |
| `SAN_STAGE1_TO_TELL_MIN_S` | 220 | s | 180 to 300 | no retune passes at cap 36 | stage 1 to the tell is quick | D.4 (i) |
| `SAN_TELL_LEAD_S` ★ | 60 | s | 45 to 90 | the lead cannot be met with the thresholds | victims are not warned long enough (Pillar 2 in spirit) | D.4 (ii) and (iv); G3 |
| `SAN_MIN_ONSET_TO_HARM_S` ★ | 300 | s | 240 to 420 | harm unreachable at cap 36 | friendly harm in a rush | D.4 (iii); the concept's "slow and costly" |
| `PENDING_MAX` | 3000 | mSU | 1500 to 6000 | the hangover outlives the danger | startles are muted | D.1; `EXP_BREACH_ENTRY` (two stacked startles fit) |

#### Exposure

| Knob | Default | Unit | Safe range | Too high | Too low | Interacts with |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `RING_EXPOSURE[1..3]` ★ | 0 / 4 / 8 | mSU per run | each < `REC_SETTLED`, non-decreasing with ring | a home deeper in the web is no refuge | depth costs nothing (Pillar 5) | D.8 (d); Web Map's ring count and `body_is_home`; AT-6 ring indexing |
| `ABOARD_EXPOSURE` ★ | 20 | mSU per run | 8 to 20 | a calm sortie feels like a drain; stage 1 in under 3 min (above 20 re-run D.4's worst-case sum) | stage 1 only after 14.3 min aboard at 8: class (b) never shows in a 30-minute session | D.8 net rates; D.8 (e); pacing hypothesis A; `AMBIENT_FLOOR` |
| `AMBIENT_FLOOR[1..3]` ★ | 62000 / 52000 / 43000 | mSU | 40000 to 68000, non-increasing, each >= 2000 from every stage threshold | stage 1 never reached from ambient | ambient reaches the tell or harm | D.8 (a) to (c); Web Map ring depth |
| `AMBIENT_MARGIN` | 5000 | mSU | 3000 to 10000 | the ambient stage ceiling is too shallow | floors approach `SAN_TELL_OFF` | D.8 (a) |
| `EXP_TELL[1..3]` ★ | 6 / 12 / 20 | mSU per run | 0 to 28 | tells dominate the descent | a tell is harmless | Attention `tier`; tier-3 TELL aboard already exceeds the cap |
| `EXP_BREACH` ★ | 28 | mSU per run | 14 to 36 | BREACH always caps | a breach is not felt | Attention `phase`; D.4 cap demonstration |
| `EXP_SUBSIDING` ★ | 14 | mSU per run | 0 to 28 | the aftermath feels like a second breach | no aftermath; the debt drains in the clear | headroom for `pending` (23 at home ring 1) |
| `EXP_BREACH_ENTRY` ★ | 1500 | mSU | 0 to 3000 (<= `PENDING_MAX`) | the hangover is long | no startle | `PENDING_MAX`; `tell_epoch` |
| `EXP_HORROR` ★ | 20 | mSU per run | 0 to 28 | the cap binds at once | horror contact is harmless | HE-32, HE-40 (Horror owns neither); D.10 |
| `HORROR_NEAR_R` ★ | 288 | px (6 tiles) | 144 to 480 | the whole room counts | contact is never registered | Horror `pos_px`; no light condition (SN-6) |
| `HORROR_ENC_BUDGET` | 3000 | mSU | 1500 to 6000 | harm reachable by about 9 encounters | a horror feels harmless after a few seconds | D.10 farm bound |
| `HORROR_ENC_RESET_S` | 120 | s | 30 to 300 | one horror is ever-binding | farmable | D.10; `REC_IDLE` |
| `HORROR_TRACK_MAX` | 4 | refs | 2 to 8 | memory | a fifth horror pays fresh | `sanity.ledger` size |
| `E4` witnessed crew death | 0 (dormant) | mSU per run | VS-B | - | - | Co-op; value TBD there |

#### Recovery

| Knob | Default | Unit | Safe range | Too high | Too low | Interacts with |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

**Tuning order for the first playtest.** (1) `ABOARD_EXPOSURE` and the three `AMBIENT_FLOOR` values decide whether class (b) appears in 30 minutes (hypothesis: stage 1 after about 5.3 min aboard in ring 1). (2) `ONSET_PM` and `LIES_MAX` then set intensity, measured by the Debug Overlay's `eligible_entries_per_min`. (3) The thresholds and the cap are touched last and only with D.4 re-run, because they carry the friendly-harm fairness.

## Visual/Audio Requirements

Sanity owns **no sprite sheets** and no sound assets. This section states WHAT must be seen and heard, for WHOM, and that every lie leaks; it does not specify shaders, easing curves, pixel widths the art bible has not fixed, or sound content (those are the Perception Filter's, the technical-artist's and the audio-director's). Every value quoted is the art bible's (cited) or a Sanity or registry value (cited). Where the bible fixes nothing, this section says so and routes the number to the Perception Filter GDD or the technical-artist lighting spike (ART 8.12) instead of inventing one. The art-director was consulted (row ids `SNV-n`, "Sanity Visual": `VA-n` is Suit's own prefix and `HR-n` the bible's). Tags: **MVP-live**; **MVP-conditional** (the bible calls for it, cost or scope confirmation owed to the producer and technical-artist); **DEFERRED(VS-A)**; **DEFERRED(VS-B)**. *(Perception Filter and Adaptive Audio & Tells are designed, `perception-filter.md` and `adaptive-audio-and-tells.md`; this section states what they must draw and play.)*

**Two things this GDD says plainly, so nobody promises too much.**

1. **MVP stage 2 is the own-cone rim fringe plus the lamp-housing glow sprite. It is not "light that lies".** The bible's stage-2 row (ART 4.4, 2.9) lists "cone appears to reach further", "shadows with no occluder", "violet-black shadows", "false reach tinted" and "apparent warmth". Every one of those except the fringe is a Lighting decoy kind (`GLOW_EXTENSION`, `PHANTOM_SHADOW`; LO-25, LO OQ 16) and is DEFERRED(VS-A) (`DECOY_OVERLAYS_MAX` 4, `DECOY_MAX_CH` 0.85, `DECOY_LEAK_PX` 2, `DECOY_LEAK_ALPHA_PM` 300, all cited and never restated). The only MVP "light lie" is the lamp-housing glow **sprite** (module-body channel, SN-16 reach): the real lamp brightness from `served_pm`, the floor light pool, `lux` and the beam radius stay true.
2. **There are two different violet fringes with two different causes; they share a look and nothing else.**

| | Own-cone rim fringe (stage 2 and up) | Crewmate-outline fringe (class c) |
|---|---|---|
| Seen by | the lying seat, on its OWN beam's rim | a viewer, on ANOTHER seat's outline and beam edges |
| Driven by | the viewer's own SIM `stage` | the subject seat's SIM `tell_on` bit (SHARED_SUMMARY); also the lie-leak on a monsterised rig (SNV-17) |
| Geometry | one continuous band tangent to the true cone rim, no root, never in a falsely extended reach | 2 art px outside the Void Steel contour, the full outer silhouette AND both edges of the beam cone (ART 5.5) |
| Motion | breathing 4 to 6 s, per viewer, must not lock to `tell_beat` | breathing 4 to 6 s (HR-7), never flicker |
| Visible to its owner? | yes, it is the diegetic self-sign (SN-14) | **no**: "a player never sees their own fringe" (ART 5.5) refers to this one only |
| Phase | MVP-conditional | DEFERRED(VS-B) |

**Strands versus fringe (Attention's resolved contention, ATT:93, AC-AT-109).** Attention's `web_strand_*` are radial, discrete, rooted at a seam, locked to `tell_beat`, global, and exist only in the true lit region. Sanity's fringe is continuous, tangential, unrooted, breathing, per viewer, and never locks to `tell_beat`. **Hue-removed still-frame test (AC-SN-154):** with hue removed, a still frame of a cone rim with strands and one with the fringe must be told apart by geometry alone. If the fringe cannot be tangential, continuous, breathing and per-viewer, that is a CONFLICT for the creative-director (ATT:93).

**Stage-2 rim fringe: state-bound, tagged MVP-conditional.** Is the fringe on whenever `stage >= 2` (state-bound) or only while a stage-2 reach lie is ACTIVE (lie-bound)? *(Orchestrator ruling, art-director position adopted, logged: state-bound.)* ART 4.4 puts the cone-rim fringe in the stage-2 row as a standing leak, every stage leaks ("subtle is allowed, absent is not", ART:73), and SN-14 relies on it as the actor's diegetic self-sign; a world-edge tell is what "look at edges" teaches, not a meter. It is nevertheless a de facto stage indicator, so the creative-director must confirm that IA-39 ("no sanity meter or number exists") tolerates it (OQ-SN-21, a user-decision item). If rejected, the fallback is lie-bound and SN-14's diegetic sign shrinks to the stage-1 gleam.

### Stage and flag transitions

| ID | Event (source) | Tag | Visual requirement | Binding rules | Channel |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| SNV-1 | Stage 0 to 1 (D.2) | MVP-live | **No screen-level event.** Stage 1 is "instruments disagree": the only visual consequence is that newly accepted class-(b) lies may show on needles, readouts and inspect cards, each with its own gleam (SNV-7). No onset effect, tint or sound sting at the boundary. | A stage change never ends a lie (SN-16); a lie vanishing at a boundary would be a tell of the boundary and uncaused pixel motion. 2.9: "must not announce itself through tempo". | n/a |

Existing lies continue unchanged. | The ramp is amplitude-monotone and must not read as a pulse or flash: PR-13 (at most 3 flashes in a rolling 1 s), HR-13 (no luminance change faster than 3 Hz), "no uncaused pixel motion" (PIPE:37). The ramp duration is the Filter's (the bible fixes none); art-director target (provisional, a tuning target and not a bible rule): slower than one fringe breath, so the onset cannot be read as the first breath. | leak |
| SNV-3 | Stage 2 to 3 | MVP-conditional (hue bleed); MVP-live (whole-sprite reach) | (a) Reach widens to the whole machine sprite (SNV-9). Stage 3 is reachable in the MVP only by sustained BREACH or horror contact (SN-29), so (b) is a late-game payoff; scope and cost confirmation owed to the producer and technical-artist (OQ-SN-22). | leak |

The flag is a SHARED_SUMMARY bit; the fringe is per-viewer presentation. | leak |

No pre-eligibility glimpses (SN-20). | Render level IS the bit (IA-31 precondition 1, AC-IA-126 half a): no rendered frame may exist in which a horror-looking crewmate has no harm plate or a plate exists on a human-looking crewmate; the swap may lag the bit by no more than one rendered frame. Recommendation: a hard cut (the bible has no dissolve vocabulary for a hallucination; a morph is a spectacle 2.9 forbids). | leak + corruptible sprite |

### Class (b), false system state (MVP-live)

Kinds by true state and stage reach come from SN-16. Reach ladder = intensity ladder: stage 1 needle, readout and inspect card (the same instrument as its wall gauge, AD-C1 resolved); stage 2 plus state glyph and lamp-housing glow sprite; stage 3 plus the whole machine sprite ("the lie extends from the gauge to the sprite", CON:246). Each lie carries the HR-5 tri-modal leak (violet gleam, glide motion, sound signature; each mode sufficient; at least two of three always present per L-L).

| ID | Lying surface | Tag | What is drawn lying | Leak | Binding rules |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| SNV-7 | Gauge needle (dial arc) | MVP-live | The needle departs the true angle at the **glide rate** (0.5 to 2 deg/s on a dial arc, ART 7.3) toward the comfort side (FALSE_OK, GLIDE on a degraded subject) or the alarm side (FALSE_FAIL, GLIDE on a healthy one), then holds the false region with a slow micro-wander (below 0.5 Hz) that never fully rests. | Hue: violet fringe `#B07BE6` on the glass or needle edge, never the core. Motion: the glide itself, the one motion no real instrument makes (slow, smooth, directional, uncaused, never at rest). Sound: a signature exists and begins no later than the glide (audio-director). | The needle never **snaps** to its false value (a jump is unsignalled change and borrows neither signature of 7.3). Honest motions stay honest: *settle* (damped spring 250 to 400 ms, overshoot at most 5 percent, then still) and *tremor* (plus or minus 1 art px, irregular, 6 to 10 Hz, returns to rest) carry no gleam and are never used by a lie. In-world gauges carry no numerals at game distance (3.1, 7.6 item 5): needle angle and zone band only. |
| SNV-8 | Digital readout and inspect card | MVP-live | Honest numerals **tick once per real change, never tween**. A lying readout ticks **continuously** as it tracks the glide, or **holds stale** while the ship audibly and visibly changes. Tick interval at least 334 ms, default `LIE_TICK_MS` 400 ms, `T_tick = max(LIE_TICK_MS, N_r x 334)` (registry: `lying_digit_tick_schedule`, `LIE_TICK_MS`; Perception Filter D.9; consecutive transitions at least 334 ms apart because 1000 / 333 = 3.003 exceeds `FLASH_MAX_PER_S` 3; the accessibility-specialist confirms, OQ-SN-23). | Gleam on the card glass; the continuous-tick or stale-hold is the motion mode; sound as SNV-7. | The inspect card is **the same instrument** as the wall gauge: it lies exactly when the gauge lies, with the same tells, from stage 1 (7.0, HR-14). The card frame stays an anchor (HR-6); violet appears only on the reading's glass, never the frame (4.6). The card is drawn only for a currently lit instrument. |
| SNV-9 | State glyph, lamp-housing glow sprite, whole machine sprite | MVP-live (stage-gated) | State glyph: the false state with the digital-readout motion mode. Lamp-housing glow sprite: the housing's emissive-APPEARANCE frame is swapped; **no `Light2D` is created or altered, the floor light pool and `lux` do not change**. Whole machine sprite (stage 3): SPRITE_SWAP shows one of the machine's own honest state frames: a rotor spinning on a stopped machine (the rotor cadence drifts and never settles to the honest rate), or a housing shown cracked on a whole one. | Gleam at the housing, glass or sprite EDGE (never a fill); the lying moving part never rests; sound as SNV-7. | **Consequence-channel firewall (L-C):** a false-broken sprite adds no scorch, no sparks-as-arc-POP, no mist, no drip, no frost, no puddle, no hull hairlines. "Housing shown cracked" is the machine's own honest broken *frame* (a `mach_*` state frame), never a `trail_*` hairline asset, because integrity hairlines along ribs are a consequence-channel trail (3.3, 5.5). A glow-sprite lie is read by the player as exposure (Principle 3), so it must be an unlit overlay frame that cannot be mistaken for a lantern pool. Real running pipes are truth. |
| SNV-10 | Own-cone rim fringe | MVP-conditional | One continuous band tangent to the TRUE cone rim, no root, breathing 4 to 6 s (per viewer). Colour `#B07BE6`, edge-only, never a fill. | Not a lie; it is the stage-2 leak and the diegetic self-sign (SN-14). | Discriminator and test above. Width: **the bible fixes none for the own-cone rim** (the 2 px of 5.5 is the crewmate outline); the Filter proposes it in the lighting spike under three constraints: it never extends the true reach, never occludes or alters rim pixels of the true beam (the true light radius is an anchor, Principle 1), and never locks to `tell_beat`. |
| SNV-11 | Lie end: EXPIRED, INVALIDATED, DEBUNKED | MVP-live | **Presentation-identical for all three end types** (the end type must not be an oracle; DEBUNKED must pass the design-test sentence: no verdict icon, card, line or cut to the truth). The needle's displayed value returns to the true value **on the glide path (at the Filter-owned return rate `GLIDE_RETURN_RATE` 3.0 deg/s, registry, not the 0.5 to 2 deg/s departure glide `GLIDE_RATE_BAND`), never a snap and never a settle** (AD-C2 resolved in SN-16). Leak amplitude is a function of the **displayed-minus-true discrepancy** (the gleam fades as the discrepancy closes), so the leak ends with the lie and no separate "lie over" event exists to read. Sprite swaps: OQ-SN-24. | A needle snapping back is uncaused motion; a *settle* would borrow the honest-change signature (7.3). After a DEBUNK the player learns the truth from the world (a hiss, a draught, a scanner **reading**), not from the interface saying so. | The glide rate is slow (0.5 to 2 deg/s), so a large dial discrepancy returns over tens of seconds during which the needle visibly disagrees after a paid scan; the Filter uses the separate, faster `GLIDE_RETURN_RATE`, still never a snap or a settle. |
| SNV-12 | `field_check` and scanner reading | MVP-live | The scanner shows an honest **reading** in the reading register (thin, light, moving marks inside a still frame, 3.4): never a verdict icon, never a real-or-fake binary, never Alarm Red or "safe green" (IA:972, F-3). No colour is "safe" in this game (4.2). | MVP scanner is an anchor (SN-19). VS-A lie-capable at stage 3 only, behind `SCANNER_LIE_STAGE`, carrying the full HR-5 tri-modal. | Truth (MVP) |
